OBSERVED OUTPUT
     1	"""Build a fresh ALFWorld/TextWorld environment for one game file, and replay actions.
     2\x09
     3	Why this file exists, and why it is shared
     4	------------------------------------------
     5	ALFWorld is interactive and multi-turn, which the platform's one-shot task contract
     6	does not express. The platform's answer to that is the tau-bench shape: the agent acts
     7	through a CLI, the CLI writes a log, and the check **replays the log in a fresh
     8	environment** and reads the environment's own verdict. For that to mean anything, the
     9	CLI and the check have to agree *exactly* on how an action becomes an observation --
    10	otherwise the trajectory the agent saw is not the trajectory the check scores, and the
    11	disagreement shows up as an inexplicable 0.
    12\x09
    13	So there is exactly one implementation of "build the environment, reset it, apply an
    14	action", and both programs import it:
    15\x09
    16	    data/datasets/alfworld/lib/gym_env.py   <- this file
    17	    data/datasets/alfworld/cli/alf          <- the agent's command line, imports this
    18	    data/datasets/alfworld/verify/check.py  <- the grade, imports this
    19\x09
    20	The adapter copies this file into the task workdir for the CLI via `SETUP`, and copies
    21	it there **again** at verification time via `VERIFY.inputs`. The second copy is what
    22	makes the check trustworthy: the agent may edit anything in its workdir, including
    23	this file, and the edit does not survive into the check.
    24\x09
    25	What "the environment's own won flag" means here
    26	-----------------------------------------------
    27	`won` is computed by TextWorld from the game's PDDL goal. It is not produced by
    28	ALFWorld, not by this file, and not by the agent. Nothing in the platform's own code
    29	decides success: `infos["won"]` is the benchmark's verdict, and the check reads it.
    30\x09
    31	The step budget
    32	---------------
    33	`STEP_BUDGET` is the number of `env.step` calls an episode may use. It is declared once
    34	and used by the CLI (which refuses action 31) and by the check (which replays at most
    35	30) and by TextWorld itself (`max_episode_steps`, which ends the episode when the
    36	budget runs out). The three have to be the same number or a trajectory could be
    37	legal for the agent and truncated for the check.
    38	"""
    39	from __future__ import annotations
    40\x09
    41	#: The community/sibling-project protocol: at most 30 actions per episode. Declared
    42	#: here rather than spelled `30` at three call sites -- see the module docstring.
    43	STEP_BUDGET = 30
    44\x09
    45	#: The file TextWorld loads. Generated by ALFWorld's own tooling and shipped in the
    46	#: `0.4.2` release asset; it embeds the PDDL domain, the task-specific grammar and the
    47	#: problem, so a game file is self-contained and does not need `$ALFWORLD_DATA/logic`.
    48	GAME_FILENAME = "game.tw-pddl"
    49\x09
    50	#: The agent's action log, one JSON object per line. It is the *only* thing the check
    51	#: reads from the agent: the check never trusts an observation, a `won` flag or a
    52	#: summary that the CLI wrote, because all of those can be forged by the agent that
    53	#: wrote them. Actions are safe to trust in a way that verdicts are not -- replaying
    54	#: them in a fresh environment is what turns them into a verdict.
    55	LOG_FILENAME = "alf_actions.jsonl"
    56\x09
    57\x09
    58	def build(game_path: str, budget: int = STEP_BUDGET):
    59	    """A fresh, single-game TextWorld environment.
    60\x09
    61	    `AlfredDemangler(shuffle=False)` is not decoration: the game file's actions use
    62	    ALFRED's demangled object names (`desk 2`, `alarmclock 1`) and TextWorld's raw names
    63	    are the mangled ones, so without the demangler every command is "not admissible".
    64	    `shuffle=False` because the benchmark's evaluation is not randomised and a shuffled
    65	    world would make two replays of one log disagree.
    66	    """
    67	    import textworld
    68	    import textworld.gym
    69	    from alfworld.agents.environment.alfred_tw_env import AlfredDemangler, AlfredInfos
    70\x09
    71	    request_infos = textworld.EnvInfos(won=True, admissible_commands=True,
    72	                                       extras=["gamefile"])
    73	    env_id = textworld.gym.register_games(
    74	        [str(game_path)], request_infos,
    75	        batch_size=1,
    76	        asynchronous=False,
    77	        max_episode_steps=budget,
    78	        wrappers=[AlfredDemangler(shuffle=False), AlfredInfos],
    79	    )
    80	    return textworld.gym.make(env_id)
    81\x09
    82\x09
    83	def reset(env) -> dict:
    84	    """The episode's first observation. `won` is read but is always false here."""
    85	    obs, infos = env.reset()
    86	    return {
    87	        "observation": obs[0],
    88	        "admissible": list(infos.get("admissible_commands", [[]])[0]),
    89	        "won": bool(infos.get("won", [False])[0]),
    90	    }
    91\x09
    92\x09
    93	def step(env, command: str) -> dict:
    94	    """Apply one command and return what TextWorld said, including its `won` flag."""
    95	    obs, _scores, dones, infos = env.step([command])
    96	    return {
    97	        "observation": obs[0],
    98	        "admissible": list(infos.get("admissible_commands", [[]])[0]),
    99	        "won": bool(infos.get("won", [False])[0]),
   100	        "done": bool(dones[0]),
   101	    }
   102\x09
   103\x09
   104	def replay(game_path: str, actions: list[str], budget: int = STEP_BUDGET) -> dict:
   105	    """Replay `actions` from a fresh episode and report the environment's own verdict.
   106\x09
   107	    Returns the initial observation, one record per action actually applied, and
   108	    `won` -- true when TextWorld reported the goal satisfied at **any** step. "At any
   109	    step" is upstream's rule, not a convenience: ALFWorld's own evaluator takes the
   110	    maximum of the per-step `won` (`alfworld/agents/eval/evaluate_dagger.py`), and an
   111	    episode that wins and then keeps acting is still a win.
   112\x09
   113	    The replay stops early when TextWorld says `done` (a win, or the budget running
   114	    out), which is what the official evaluator does too.
   115	    """
   116	    env = build(game_path, budget)
   117	    try:
   118	        first = reset(env)
   119	        records: list[dict] = []
   120	        won = first["won"]
   121	        for action in list(actions)[:budget]:
   122	            record = dict(step(env, action))
   123	            record["action"] = action
   124	            records.append(record)
   125	            won = won or record["won"]
   126	            if record["done"]:
   127	                break
   128	        return {"initial_observation": first["observation"],
   129	                "initial_admissible": first["admissible"],
   130	                "records": records, "won": won}
   131	    finally:
   132	        try:
   133	            env.close()
   134	        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - a closed env is not a measurement failure
   135	            pass
   136\x09
   137\x09
   138	def action_log_records(path) -> list[dict]:
   139	    """Parse an action log defensively. A line this cannot read is not an action.
   140\x09
   141	    Kept here rather than in the check so the CLI and the check read the log the same
   142	    way. A malformed line is dropped rather than raising: the log is the agent's file,
   143	    and a truncated write must not turn into a check that cannot run at all -- it turns
   144	    into a shorter episode, which the check then scores honestly.
   145	    """
   146	    import json
   147	    from pathlib import Path
   148\x09
   149	    p = Path(path)
   150	    if not p.is_file():
   151	        return []
   152	    out = []
   153	    for line in p.read_text(errors="replace").splitlines():
   154	        line = line.strip()
   155	        if not line:
   156	            continue
   157	        try:
   158	            record = json.loads(line)
   159	        except ValueError:
   160	            continue
   161	        if isinstance(record, dict):
   162	            out.append(record)
   163	    return out
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""The ALFWorld check: replay the agent's actions in a **fresh** environment and read
     3	the environment's own `won` flag.
     4\x09
     5	This is the dataset's whole grade. It does not read a summary the agent wrote, it does
     6	not read the agent's observation, and it does not read any `won` the agent's CLI
     7	recorded -- all of those are the agent's own bytes and the agent can make them say
     8	anything. It reads one thing from the agent: the ordered list of actions in
     9	`alf_actions.jsonl`. It then rebuilds the game from a trusted copy of the game file,
    10	applies those actions one at a time to TextWorld, and asks TextWorld whether the PDDL
    11	goal is satisfied. Success is `infos["won"]`, exactly as
    12	`alfworld/agents/eval/evaluate_dagger.py` reads it.
    13\x09
    14	The rule, precisely
    15	-------------------
    16	    reward = 1  iff  the replayed episode reports `won` at some step
    17	    reward = 0  otherwise
    18\x09
    19	"At some step" is upstream's rule, not a convenience: ALFWorld's evaluator takes the
    20	maximum of the per-step `won` scores over the episode. An episode that satisfies the
    21	goal and then keeps acting is still a success, and the replay stops at the first `done`
    22	(TextWorld's own end-of-episode signal) exactly as the evaluator's loop does.
    23\x09
    24	**`alf done` is recorded but is not a gate.** tau-bench's check *must* require `tau
    25	done`, because tau-bench itself only computes a reward for a finished episode. ALFWorld
    26	has no such rule: an episode ends when TextWorld says it ends (the goal is met or the
    27	step budget is spent), and nothing in the benchmark asks the agent to declare anything.
    28	Adding a `done` gate here would be inventing a requirement the benchmark does not have
    29	-- which is a different failure from dropping one, and just as misleading. The record
    30	still carries whether `done` was declared, because it is useful evidence.
    31\x09
    32	What makes this check trustworthy
    33	---------------------------------
    34	* The game file is re-materialized by the platform at verification time
    35	  (`VERIFY.inputs`), so editing `<workdir>/game.tw-pddl` does not change the grade.
    36	* The same is true of this file and of `gym_env.py`: `SETUP` ships them for the agent's
    37	  own CLI, and verification overwrites them. The check imports `gym_env` from the
    38	  workdir *after* that overwrite.
    39	* An episode the check cannot measure -- a game that will not load, a game file that is
    40	  missing -- writes **no reward file** and exits 3. The platform then reports the task
    41	  as unmeasured rather than as a zero the agent earned. A bare 0 is indistinguishable
    42	  from a wrong answer, and that distinction is the whole reason this file writes
    43	  evidence.
    44\x09
    45	Outputs
    46	-------
    47	    reward file    1 or 0   (`/verifier/reward.txt` by default)
    48	    evidence file  JSON     why, per action: what happened, which step won, what the
    49	                            official per-step reduction would have produced
    50	    stdout         why, in one line, so the platform's verdict detail carries it too
    51	"""
    52	from __future__ import annotations
    53\x09
    54	import argparse
    55	import json
    56	import os
    57	import sys
    58	import traceback
    59	from pathlib import Path
    60\x09
    61	# Resolve helpers beside trusted code, including in the sealed /scorer root.
    62	sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
    63	from data.scaffolds.scorer_input import add_arguments, run_bound
    64\x09
    65	import gym_env  # noqa: E402
    66\x09
    67	#: The container contract's locations. On a host (`files`) environment these are
    68	#: overridden by the flags below, because `/verifier` does not exist there.
    69	DEFAULT_REWARD_FILE = "/verifier/reward.txt"
    70	DEFAULT_EVIDENCE_FILE = "/verifier/evidence.json"
    71\x09
    72\x09
    73	def parse_args(argv):
    74	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
    75	    parser.add_argument("--workdir", default=os.environ.get("ALFWORLD_WORKDIR", "."))
    76	    parser.add_argument("--game", default=None, help="the trusted game file")
    77	    parser.add_argument("--log", default=None, help="the agent's action log")
    78	    parser.add_argument("--reward-file",
    79	                        default=os.environ.get("ALFWORLD_REWARD_FILE",
    80	                                               DEFAULT_REWARD_FILE))
    81	    parser.add_argument("--evidence-file",
    82	                        default=os.environ.get("ALFWORLD_EVIDENCE_FILE",
    83	                                               DEFAULT_EVIDENCE_FILE))
    84	    parser.add_argument("--budget", type=int, default=gym_env.STEP_BUDGET)
    85	    add_arguments(parser)
    86	    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    87	    workdir = Path(args.workdir)
    88	    args.game = Path(args.game) if args.game else workdir / gym_env.GAME_FILENAME
    89	    args.log = Path(args.log) if args.log else workdir / gym_env.LOG_FILENAME
    90	    args.reward_file = Path(args.reward_file)
    91	    args.evidence_file = Path(args.evidence_file)
    92	    return args
    93\x09
    94\x09
    95	def write_reward(path: Path, score: int) -> None:
    96	    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    97	    path.write_text(f"{score}\n")
    98\x09
    99\x09
   100	def write_evidence(path: Path, evidence: dict) -> None:
   101	    """Evidence is for a reader, never for the score: failing to write it is not fatal."""
   102	    try:
   103	        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   104	        path.write_text(json.dumps(evidence, indent=1, sort_keys=True,
   105	                                   ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
   106	    except OSError as exc:
   107	        print(f"could not write evidence: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
   108\x09
   109\x09
   110	def official_reduction(won_per_step: list[bool]) -> float:
   111	    """The per-episode score ALFWorld's own evaluator computes from a `won` sequence.
   112\x09
   113	    Copied from `alfworld/agents/eval/evaluate_dagger.py`, which builds
   114\x09
   115	        step_rewards = [curr - prev for curr, prev in zip(scores, prev_rewards)]
   116	        ...
   117	        game_points = np.max(np.array(sequence_game_points), 0).tolist()
   118\x09
   119	    with `scores = [float(item) for item in infos["won"]]`. Because `won` is monotone
   120	    (TextWorld does not un-win a game) and the sequence starts at 0.0, the maximum of
   121	    the increments equals "did any step win", which is what this file returns. The
   122	    reduction is computed here anyway so the evidence carries both numbers, and
   123	    `tests/quality/test_alfworld_official_scorer.py` pins the equality against a
   124	    verbatim copy of upstream's code -- the copy, not an import of their package.
   125	    """
   126	    prev = 0.0
   127	    increments: list[float] = []
   128	    for won in won_per_step:
   129	        curr = 1.0 if won else 0.0
   130	        increments.append(curr - prev)
   131	        prev = curr
   132	    return max(increments) if increments else 0.0
   133\x09
   134\x09
   135	def main(argv) -> int:
   136	    args = parse_args(argv)
   137	    return run_bound(args, {str(args.game): args.game},
   138	                     lambda inputs, receipt: _score(args, inputs, receipt))
   139\x09
   140\x09
   141	def _score(args, inputs, receipt) -> int:
   142	    evidence: dict = {
   143	        "scorer_input": receipt,
   144	        "game_file": str(args.game),
   145	        "action_log": str(args.log),
   146	        "reward_file": str(args.reward_file),
   147	    }
   148\x09
   149	    if not args.game.is_file():
   150	        # The check's own input is missing: not a measurement.
   151	        evidence["error"] = f"no game file at {args.game}"
   152	        evidence["measured"] = False
   153	        write_evidence(args.evidence_file, evidence)
   154	        print(f"check cannot run: {evidence['error']}", file=sys.stderr)
   155	        return 3
   156\x09
   157	    records = gym_env.action_log_records(args.log)
   158	    actions = [str(r["action"]) for r in records
   159	               if r.get("kind") == "action" and isinstance(r.get("action"), str)]
   160	    declared_done = any(r.get("kind") == "done" for r in records)
   161	    unparseable = len([line for line in
   162	                       (args.log.read_text(errors="replace").splitlines()
   163	                        if args.log.is_file() else []) if line.strip()]) - len(records)
   164	    evidence.update(actions_in_log=len(actions), log_records=len(records),
   165	                    unparseable_lines=unparseable, agent_declared_done=declared_done,
   166	                    budget=args.budget)
   167\x09
   168	    try:
   169	        state = gym_env.replay(str(inputs[str(args.game)]), actions, args.budget)
   170	    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a check that cannot run is not a zero
   171	        evidence["error"] = f"could not build or replay the environment: {exc}"
   172	        evidence["traceback"] = traceback.format_exc()[-2000:]
   173	        evidence["measured"] = False
   174	        write_evidence(args.evidence_file, evidence)
   175	        print(f"check cannot run: {evidence['error']}", file=sys.stderr)
   176	        return 3
   177\x09
   178	    won_per_step = [bool(r["won"]) for r in state["records"]]
   179	    won_step = next((i for i, w in enumerate(won_per_step) if w), None)
   180	    replayed = state["records"]
   181	    budget_exhausted = (len(actions) > len(replayed)
   182	                        and not (replayed and replayed[-1]["done"]))
   183	    # A command TextWorld did not understand comes back as "Nothing happens." It still
   184	    # cost a step, and recording it is how a reader tells "the agent tried and was
   185	    # refused" apart from "the agent did nothing".
   186	    no_ops = [r["action"] for r in replayed
   187	              if "Nothing happens" in str(r.get("observation", ""))]
   188	    reward = 1 if state["won"] else 0
   189	    official = official_reduction(won_per_step)
   190\x09
   191	    evidence.update(
   192	        measured=True,
   193	        reward=reward,
   194	        won=bool(state["won"]),
   195	        won_at_step=won_step,
   196	        actions_replayed=len(replayed),
   197	        actions_not_replayed=len(actions) - len(replayed),
   198	        budget_exhausted=bool(budget_exhausted),
   199	        episode_done=bool(replayed and replayed[-1]["done"]),
   200	        no_op_actions=no_ops,
   201	        won_per_step=won_per_step,
   202	        official_game_points=official,
   203	        # The agent's own narrative is not trusted, so it is not included. What is
   204	        # included is the *action* at each step and TextWorld's answer, which is the
   205	        # material a reader needs to see why the reward is what it is.
   206	        steps=[{"step": i, "action": r["action"], "won": bool(r["won"]),
   207	                "done": bool(r["done"]),
   208	                "observation": str(r.get("observation", ""))[:200]}
   209	               for i, r in enumerate(replayed)],
   210	    )
   211\x09
   212	    if won_step is None:
   213	        if budget_exhausted:
   214	            why = (f"the goal was not satisfied in the {args.budget}-step budget; the "
   215	                   f"log holds {len(actions)} actions and only {len(replayed)} replayed")
   216	        elif not actions:
   217	            why = "the log holds no actions, so the goal cannot have been satisfied"
   218	        else:
   219	            why = (f"the goal was not satisfied after {len(replayed)} action(s); the "
   220	                   f"episode ended without a win")
   221	    else:
   222	        why = f"the goal was satisfied at step {won_step + 1} of {len(replayed)}"
   223	    evidence["why"] = why
   224	    write_evidence(args.evidence_file, evidence)
   225\x09
   226	    if reward == 1:
   227	        write_reward(args.reward_file, 1)
   228	        print(f"reward 1: {why} (official game points {official:g}; "
   229	              f"agent declared done={declared_done})")
   230	        return 0
   231\x09
   232	    write_reward(args.reward_file, 0)
   233	    print(f"reward 0: {why} (official game points {official:g}; "
   234	          f"no-op actions {len(no_ops)})")
   235	    return 1
   236\x09
   237\x09
   238	if __name__ == "__main__":
   239	    try:
   240	        raise SystemExit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
   241	    except SystemExit:
   242	        raise
   243	    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - a crash must not look like a measured zero
   244	        traceback.print_exc()
   245	        raise SystemExit(4)
     1	"""ALFWorld as a dataset: a text-adventure benchmark whose reward is PDDL goal
     2	satisfaction computed by the environment. INTERFACE.md §2.5.9.
     3\x09
     4	    python3 tools/datasets.py --write        # regenerate DOWNLOADS.md from downloads()
     5	    python3 data/datasets/alfworld/control.py --oracle   # the oracle control (no Docker)
     6	    driver.py --dataset alfworld ...
     7\x09
     8	Where the tasks come from
     9	-------------------------
    10	`alfworld/alfworld`, the `valid_unseen` split of the `json_2.1.1` data. That is the
    11	split ALFWorld's own paper evaluates out-of-distribution on, and it is what a score
    12	here should be compared with. The task list is **not** this adapter's invention: it is
    13	the same filter `alfworld/agents/environment/alfred_tw_env.py:collect_game_files`
    14	applies -- a trial directory must have both `traj_data.json` and `game.tw-pddl`, must
    15	not be a movable-receptacle or sliced-object trajectory, and its `game.tw-pddl` must
    16	carry `solvable: true`. Measured over the extracted data: 134 trials pass, which is
    17	ALFWorld's own `Evaluating with 134 games`.
    18\x09
    19	The three provisioning traps, each verified rather than repeated
    20	---------------------------------------------------------------
    21	1. **The data is in GitHub release assets, not in the repository and not on Hugging
    22	   Face.** The filenames and the release tags come from ALFWorld's own
    23	   `scripts/alfworld-download`; `downloads()` below names them with the tags pinned.
    24	2. **`solvable` lives only in `game.tw-pddl`, which ships in the `0.4.2` asset**
    25	   (`json_2.1.3_tw-pddl.zip`). Verified: `json_2.1.1_json.zip` contains only
    26	   `traj_data.json` (7302 of them) and `json_2.1.1_pddl.zip` only `initial_state.pddl`
    27	   (7080); neither contains a `game.tw-pddl`. Without that key
    28	   `collect_game_files` skips **every** trial and the dataset loads as empty.
    29	   Measured nuance: because the `0.4.2` game file embeds `pddl_problem` and `grammar`,
    30	   the `json_2.1.1_pddl.zip` archive is *not* read by the loading path -- it is read by
    31	   ALFWorld's generation path (`alfworld-generate`). `downloads()` still names all
    32	   three, because `alfworld-download` fetches all three and a regeneration needs it.
    33	3. **The PDDL domain and the TextWorld grammar are in the repository's Python package,
    34	   not in any data pack.** Verified location:
    35	   `alfworld/data/alfred.pddl` and `alfworld/data/alfred.twl2` -- i.e.
    36	   `_src/repo/alfworld/data/` in the checkout this adapter was validated against, and
    37	   `site-packages/alfworld/data/` in an install. ALFWorld's own
    38	   `scripts/alfworld-download` copies them from the *installed package* into
    39	   `$ALFWORLD_DATA/logic/`, which is what `base_config.yaml` and `eval_config.yaml`
    40	   point `logic.domain`/`logic.grammar` at. The symptom of a missing one depends on
    41	   where you enter: pointing TextWorld at a `.pddl` (or extensionless) file gives
    42	   `ValueError: Unsupported game format`, while an empty grammar gives a bare
    43	   `StopIteration` and a missing `--domain` gives `FileNotFoundError` -- none of which
    44	   names the real cause. Our loading path does not need them (the game file is
    45	   self-contained), and the adapter does not depend on the `logic/` copy; it is
    46	   provisioned because ALFWorld's own tooling expects it.
    47\x09
    48	How ALFWorld fits the platform's one-shot contract
    49	--------------------------------------------------
    50	ALFWorld is interactive and multi-turn; the platform grades what a harness *left
    51	behind*. The tau-bench pattern resolves that: the agent acts through a CLI
    52	(`data/datasets/alfworld/cli/alf`), the CLI appends every action to `alf_actions.jsonl`, and
    53	`data/datasets/alfworld/verify/check.py` replays that log in a freshly built environment and
    54	reads TextWorld's own `won` flag. The CLI's observations, its summary and any `won` it
    55	prints are never consulted -- the log holds actions and actions are re-executed.
    56\x09
    57	The environment is `kind: "files"`, not a container. That is forced by a hard constraint
    58	of this exercise (`Do NOT use Docker`) and it is the reason this dataset works on a
    59	host with nothing but `pip install alfworld textworld`: the CLI and the check both run
    60	under the platform's Python. The consequence is stated loudly in
    61	`docs/dataset-onboarding-skill.md`: a `files` environment cannot declare a longer
    62	`agent_timeout_s`, and the platform's `reward_file`/`evidence_file` channels were
    63	container-only before this dataset (`eval/verification/verifier.py::_verify` now honours them for a
    64	host check as well -- the smallest change that let a no-Docker dataset keep the
    65	contract's evidence channel).
    66\x09
    67	The metric, and what is deliberately not added
    68	----------------------------------------------
    69	`reward = 1` iff the replayed episode reports `won` at some step inside `STEP_BUDGET`
    70	actions. This is ALFWorld's own metric (`evaluate_dagger` takes the maximum of the
    71	per-step `won`). `alf done` exists in the CLI -- the harness needs a way to stop -- but
    72	the check does **not** require it: ALFWorld has no such requirement, and tau-bench's
    73	`done` gate exists only because tau-bench computes a reward only for a finished
    74	episode. Adding one here would be inventing a requirement the benchmark does not have.
    75\x09
    76	The split
    77	---------
    78	The benchmark's own evaluation split is *all* of `valid_unseen` and it declares no
    79	train/eval division. The platform needs one, so it is **imposed here**, derived from the
    80	task id by hash so that adding or removing a task does not reshuffle the other side
    81	(`_is_eval`, the same rule `data/datasets/terminal_bench.py` uses). A score on the eval side is
    82	a score on about a third of `valid_unseen`, not on the benchmark; `HG_ALFWORLD_SPLIT=none`
    83	runs every task as eval and accepts the driver's warning.
    84\x09
    85	Noise, before anyone compares two ALFWorld runs
    86	-----------------------------------------------
    87	Measured on this machine with `qwen3.5-9b-local`, the same arm, the same 20 tasks, the
    88	same seed and temperature 0, over five batches: **10 / 8 / 11 / 11 / 11 tasks, SD 1.30
    89	tasks = 6.5 percentage points, 4 of 20 tasks changed side at least once.** A move smaller
    90	than roughly 6 points on 20 tasks is the noise. The full note is in
    91	`docs/experiment_noise.md`; quote the task count with every ALFWorld number, because the
    92	granularity is `1/n`.
    93	"""
    94\x09
    95	from __future__ import annotations
    96\x09
    97	from data.scaffolds.scorer_input import bind as bind_scorer_input
    98\x09
    99	#: See `data/scaffolds/registry.py:KIND_DATASET`. A fixture is platform test material and is
   100	#: deliberately not offered as a choice in the Launch form.
   101	KIND = "dataset"
   102\x09
   103	#: The oracle control submits the benchmark's own released reference; see
   104	#: `data/dataset.schema.json` (`module.oracle_about`) for what the other value means.
   105	ORACLE_SOURCE = "released"
   106\x09
   107\x09
   108	#: The benchmark's own evaluation set, within this adapter's pool: **every task here belongs
   109	#: to it**. The pool is `valid_unseen`, which is exactly ALFWorld's own evaluation split (134 tasks). The benchmark's 3,553-task `train` set is not loaded, so this pool holds no study material.
   110	#:
   111	#: `None` is the accurate value only because nothing in the pool is study material -- it does
   112	#: not claim the pool is the whole of the benchmark's evaluation set (see `ds1000`, which is a
   113	#: measured subset). `--split official --side eval` is the comparable run: the whole pool, with
   114	#: no study material in it. `--split declared` keeps this adapter's own division.
   115	OFFICIAL_EVAL = None
   116\x09
   117	import hashlib
   118	import json
   119	import os
   120	import re
   121	from pathlib import Path
   122\x09
   123	#: Where the data lives. Outside the platform tree on purpose: dataset content is not
   124	#: platform code, is never committed, and is what `tools/datasets.py` provisions.
   125	#: `HG_ALFWORLD_DATA` overrides it so a reader can point at another copy.
   126	DATASET_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("HG_ALFWORLD_DATA",
   127	                                   "/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/datasets/alfworld"))
   128\x09
   129	#: The split this dataset is. `valid_seen` and `train` are on disk too; only the
   130	#: out-of-distribution evaluation split is used, which is the one ALFWorld reports.
   131	DATA_SUBDIR = ("json_2.1.1", "valid_unseen")
   132\x09
   133	#: This module's own directory: the adapter is `data/datasets/alfworld.py`, its content is
   134	#: `data/datasets/alfworld/`.
   135	ASSETS = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "alfworld"
   136	CLI_DIR = ASSETS / "cli"
   137	LIB_DIR = ASSETS / "lib"
   138	VERIFY_DIR = ASSETS / "verify"
   139\x09
   140	#: The step budget and the two filenames are **declared once**, in
   141	#: `data/datasets/alfworld/lib/gym_env.py`, which the agent's CLI and the check both import.
   142	#: Repeating `30` here would be a second declaration that can drift from the one the
   143	#: environment is built with -- and a drift there means an action is legal for the agent
   144	#: and truncated for the check. Loaded by file path rather than by `import gym_env`,
   145	#: because `lib/` is not on `sys.path` and putting it there from inside a dataset module
   146	#: would leak a generic module name into the driver's interpreter.
   147	def _load_gym_env():
   148	    import importlib.util
   149	    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("alfworld_gym_env",
   150	                                                  LIB_DIR / "gym_env.py")
   151	    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
   152	    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
   153	    return module
   154\x09
   155\x09
   156	_GYM_ENV = _load_gym_env()
   157\x09
   158	#: The community/sibling-project protocol: at most 30 actions per episode.
   159	STEP_BUDGET = _GYM_ENV.STEP_BUDGET
   160	GAME_IN_WORKDIR = _GYM_ENV.GAME_FILENAME
   161	LOG_IN_WORKDIR = _GYM_ENV.LOG_FILENAME
   162\x09
   163	#: Where the check is told to put its verdict. The absolute paths are the container
   164	#: contract; on a `files` environment the platform resolves them against the workdir
   165	#: (`eval/verification/verifier.py::_verify`), and the check's `argv` passes the same location in the
   166	#: relative spelling its own working directory understands.
   167	REWARD_FILE = "/verifier/reward.txt"
   168	EVIDENCE_FILE = "/verifier/evidence.json"
   169	REWARD_REL = "verifier/reward.txt"
   170	EVIDENCE_REL = "verifier/evidence.json"
   171\x09
   172	#: ALFWorld's task-type vocabulary. A `traj_data.json` whose `task_type` is not in this
   173	#: set is not a task ALFWorld evaluates, so it is not one this dataset returns.
   174	TASK_TYPES = {
   175	    1: "pick_and_place_simple",
   176	    2: "look_at_obj_in_light",
   177	    3: "pick_clean_then_place_in_recep",
   178	    4: "pick_heat_then_place_in_recep",
   179	    5: "pick_cool_then_place_in_recep",
   180	    6: "pick_two_obj_and_place",
   181	}
   182\x09
   183	#: The templated goal lives in the game's TextWorld grammar, as the `rhs` of the `task`
   184	#: rule. The grammar is TWL2 (JSON with `#` comments), so it cannot be `json.loads`-ed;
   185	#: the rule is pulled out with a regex and unescaped as a JSON string. Verified on all
   186	#: 134 valid_unseen games.
   187	_GOAL_RE = re.compile(r'"task"\s*:\s*\[\s*\{\s*"rhs"\s*:\s*"((?:[^"\\]|\\.)*)"')
   188\x09
   189	#: The one field of a game file that must never reach the agent. `walkthrough` is
   190	#: ALFWorld's own reference solution for the game -- a list of the exact commands that
   191	#: solve it. The game file is the task's initial state, so it is handed to the agent;
   192	#: handing over the walkthrough would hand over the answer. `SETUP` and every verifier
   193	#: input therefore carry `_stripped_game()` rather than the file on disk, and the
   194	#: oracle control reads the original from the data root.
   195	_LEAKED_FIELD = "walkthrough"
   196\x09
   197\x09
   198	def _split_dir() -> Path:
   199	    return DATASET_ROOT.joinpath(*DATA_SUBDIR)
   200\x09
   201\x09
   202	def _collect() -> list[dict]:
   203	    """Every runnable game in the split, filtered exactly as ALFWorld filters them.
   204\x09
   205	    The filter is `AlfredTWEnv.collect_game_files`, reproduced rather than called:
   206	    calling it would drag in `textworld` and crawl the whole data tree at import time,
   207	    and the adapter must be importable on a machine that has not provisioned the data
   208	    (see `published()` in `data/scaffolds/registry.py`: a module that will not import is hidden
   209	    from the chooser). The rules, in the same order upstream applies them:
   210\x09
   211	      * a trial directory must hold `traj_data.json` and `game.tw-pddl`;
   212	      * its path must not contain `movable` or `Sliced` (unsupported trajectories);
   213	      * `traj_data.json`'s `task_type` must be one ALFWorld evaluates;
   214	      * `game.tw-pddl` must carry `solvable` and it must be true.
   215\x09
   216	    Each returned record is everything the adapter needs and nothing it does not.
   217	    """
   218	    root = _split_dir()
   219	    records: list[dict] = []
   220	    for game_path in sorted(root.glob("*/*/game.tw-pddl")):
   221	        trial_dir = game_path.parent
   222	        task_dir = trial_dir.parent
   223	        label = f"{task_dir.name}/{trial_dir.name}"
   224	        if "movable" in str(trial_dir) or "Sliced" in str(trial_dir):
   225	            continue
   226	        traj_path = trial_dir / "traj_data.json"
   227	        if not traj_path.is_file():
   228	            continue
   229	        try:
   230	            traj_data = json.loads(traj_path.read_text())
   231	            gamedata = json.loads(game_path.read_text())
   232	        except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
   233	            raise ValueError(f"alfworld: {label} is unreadable: {exc}") from exc
   234	        if traj_data.get("task_type") not in TASK_TYPES.values():
   235	            continue
   236	        if "solvable" not in gamedata:
   237	            # The 0.4.2 asset is the only one with this key; an extraction that skipped
   238	            # it lands here for every trial. Say that rather than "0 tasks".
   239	            raise ValueError(
   240	                f"alfworld: {label} has no `solvable` key -- the game files are not the "
   241	                f"0.4.2 `json_2.1.3_tw-pddl.zip` ones. See downloads() and "
   242	                f"tools/datasets.py --check")
   243	        if not gamedata["solvable"]:
   244	            continue
   245	        match = _GOAL_RE.search(str(gamedata.get("grammar") or ""))
   246	        if not match:
   247	            raise ValueError(
   248	                f"alfworld: {label}: the game grammar has no `task` rule, so the "
   249	                f"goal text cannot be read")
   250	        goal = json.loads('"' + match.group(1) + '"')
   251	        records.append({
   252	            # The official per-game label is the last two path components
   253	            # (`evaluate_dagger` prints `"/".join(game_names[i].split("/")[-3:-1])`).
   254	            # A slash is not safe in a platform task id (it becomes a container name
   255	            # and a path component), so it is spelled `__` here.
   256	            "task_id": f"{task_dir.name}__{trial_dir.name}",
   257	            "label": label,
   258	            "task_type": traj_data["task_type"],
   259	            "goal": goal,
   260	            "game_path": game_path,
   261	            "stripped_game": _stripped_game(gamedata),
   262	        })
   263	    return records
   264\x09
   265\x09
   266	def _stripped_game(gamedata: dict) -> str:
   267	    """The game file without the reference solution, as JSON text.
   268\x09
   269	    Kept as a string because that is the shape `SETUP`/`VERIFY.inputs` take for inline
   270	    content, and because it means the stripped copy is built once at import instead of
   271	    being written somewhere on disk that could drift from its source.
   272	    """
   273	    clean = {k: v for k, v in gamedata.items() if k != _LEAKED_FIELD}
   274	    return json.dumps(clean)
   275\x09
   276\x09
   277	def _goal(record: dict) -> str:
   278	    """The instruction, with a preamble that names only the interface.
   279\x09
   280	    Nothing here hints at the solution. It states the goal the environment will check,
   281	    how to act, and the budget -- the three things the agent cannot discover from the
   282	    interface alone.
   283	    """
   284	    return (
   285	        f"# Task\n"
   286	        f"\n"
   287	        f"You are in a text-based house, described one room at a time, and you act by\n"
   288	        f"typing commands. The game is `{GAME_IN_WORKDIR}` in your working directory.\n"
   289	        f"\n"
   290	        f"**{record['goal']}**\n"
   291	        f"\n"
   292	        f"## How to act\n"
   293	        f"\n"
   294	        f"- Look at where you are and what you may do:\n"
   295	        f"      python3 alf look\n"
   296	        f"- Take one action (it must be one of the admissible actions `alf look`\n"
   297	        f"  prints; quote it):\n"
   298	        f"      python3 alf do \"go to desk 2\"\n"
   299	        f"- When the task is finished, say so:\n"
   300	        f"      python3 alf done\n"
   301	        f"- Every action is appended to `{LOG_IN_WORKDIR}` and costs one of your\n"
   302	        f"  **{STEP_BUDGET}** steps. A step that TextWorld does not understand still\n"
   303	        f"  costs a step.\n"
   304	        f"\n"
   305	        f"## How it is scored\n"
   306	        f"\n"
   307	        f"- Your actions are replayed from the beginning in a fresh environment, and the\n"
   308	        f"  task counts as solved only if the environment's own goal check reports the\n"
   309	        f"  goal satisfied within the {STEP_BUDGET}-step budget. There is no judge and\n"
   310	        f"  no partial credit.\n"
   311	        f"- Nothing you print is read. Only the actions you take are.\n"
   312	    )
   313\x09
   314\x09
   315	def _setup(record: dict) -> dict:
   316	    """What the agent receives before it starts.
   317\x09
   318	    The CLI and its shared environment library travel as `from` references into this
   319	    repository; the game travels as inline content because it must be the *stripped*
   320	    copy (see `_LEAKED_FIELD`), which is not the bytes on disk.
   321	    """
   322	    return {"files": [
   323	        {"path": GAME_IN_WORKDIR, "content": record["stripped_game"]},
   324	        {"path": "gym_env.py", "from": str(LIB_DIR / "gym_env.py")},
   325	        {"path": "alf", "from": str(CLI_DIR / "alf")},
   326	    ]}
   327\x09
   328\x09
   329	def _verifier(record: dict) -> dict:
   330	    """The grade: the check, its trusted game, and where the verdict goes.
   331\x09
   332	    `inputs` use `path` (relative to the workdir), not `dst`: on a `files` environment
   333	    the platform materialises these into the workdir, and `dst` is a container path.
   334	    The check, the game and the environment library are re-materialised at verification
   335	    time, so nothing the agent wrote to any of them is what runs.
   336\x09
   337	    `reward_file`/`evidence_file` are absolute in-environment paths -- the container
   338	    contract. On a `files` environment `eval/verification/verifier.py` resolves them against the
   339	    workdir, and the `argv` flags below write to that same resolved location. Note the
   340	    **top-level-in-the-container** rule from `data/datasets/tau_bench.py:_verifier` does not
   341	    apply here (there is no `docker cp`), but the paths are kept absolute anyway so the
   342	    adapter is a valid container dataset if one is ever built.
   343	    """
   344	    spec = {
   345	        "kind": "command",
   346	        "argv": [
   347	            "python3", "check.py",
   348	            "--workdir", ".",
   349	            "--game", GAME_IN_WORKDIR,
   350	            "--log", LOG_IN_WORKDIR,
   351	            "--reward-file", REWARD_REL,
   352	            "--evidence-file", EVIDENCE_REL,
   353	            "--budget", str(STEP_BUDGET),
   354	        ],
   355	        "inputs": [
   356	            {"path": "check.py", "from": str(VERIFY_DIR / "check.py")},
   357	            {"path": "gym_env.py", "from": str(LIB_DIR / "gym_env.py")},
   358	            {"path": GAME_IN_WORKDIR, "content": record["stripped_game"]},
   359	        ],
   360	        "reward_file": REWARD_FILE,
   361	        "evidence_file": EVIDENCE_FILE,
   362	        "timeout_s": 300,
   363	    }
   364	    from data.runtimes import NATIVE_PINS
   365	    spec["native_runtime"] = NATIVE_PINS["alfworld"].copy()
   366	    return bind_scorer_input(spec, record["task_id"], [GAME_IN_WORKDIR])
   367\x09
   368\x09

   462\x09
   463\x09
   464	def provision_status() -> dict:
   465	    """What is present locally, for `tools/datasets.py --check`.
   466\x09
   467	    Deliberately does not import `textworld` or build an environment: this is the
   468	    answer to "did the provisioning run", which has to work on a machine where it did
   469	    not.
   470	    """
   471	    split = _split_dir()
   472	    games = sorted(split.glob("*/*/game.tw-pddl")) if split.is_dir() else []
   473	    solvable = 0
   474	    for game in games:
   475	        try:
   476	            if "solvable" in json.loads(game.read_text()):
   477	                solvable += 1
   478	        except (OSError, ValueError):
   479	            continue
   480	    logic = DATASET_ROOT / "logic"
   481	    return {
   482	        "root": str(DATASET_ROOT),
   483	        "present": bool(games),
   484	        "split_dir": str(split),
   485	        "game_files": len(games),
   486	        "with_solvable": solvable,
   487	        "logic_files": sorted(p.name for p in logic.glob("*")) if logic.is_dir() else [],
   488	        "tasks": len(_RECORDS),
   489	    }
   490\x09
   491\x09
   492	#: Read once at import. `load()` is called more than once per run and the content is
   493	#: immutable. A missing data root is **not** an import failure: `published()` catches
   494	#: exceptions and hides a module that will not import, and a dataset that cannot be
   495	#: seen in the chooser before its data is provisioned is a dataset a reader cannot
   496	#: find the instructions for. `_LOAD_ERROR` carries the reason into `load()`.
   497	try:
   498	    _RECORDS: list[dict] = _collect()
   499	    _LOAD_ERROR: str | None = None
   500	except Exception as _exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - reported by load(), which is where it bites
   501	    _RECORDS = []
   502	    _LOAD_ERROR = f"{type(_exc).__name__}: {_exc}"
   503\x09
   504\x09
   505	def load() -> tuple[list[dict], dict[str, str]]:
   506	    """`(tasks, scorable)`. Every task is graded by its `VERIFY` entry, so the
   507	    `scorable` map is present only because the interface asks for it."""
   508	    if _LOAD_ERROR is not None:
   509	        raise ValueError(f"alfworld: cannot read {_split_dir()} ({_LOAD_ERROR}). "
   510	                         f"Provision it with `python3 tools/datasets.py --list`.")
   511	    if not _RECORDS:
   512	        raise ValueError(
   513	            f"alfworld: no runnable game under {_split_dir()}. The usual cause is the "
   514	            f"0.4.2 `json_2.1.3_tw-pddl.zip` not being extracted, which leaves every "
   515	            f"trial without `game.tw-pddl`. See downloads() and "
   516	            f"`python3 tools/datasets.py --check`.")
   517	    tasks = [{"task_id": r["task_id"], "goal": _goal(r), "inputs": []}
   518	             for r in _RECORDS]
   519	    return tasks, {r["task_id"]: "" for r in _RECORDS}
   520\x09
   521\x09
   522	def _is_eval(task_id: str) -> bool:
   523	    """A deterministic third, by hash of the task id.
   524\x09
   525	    Hashed rather than sliced off the sorted list so that adding or removing a task
   526	    does not reshuffle which side every other task is on -- a split that moves under
   527	    the benchmark is a split whose past scores cannot be compared with its future ones.
   528	    """
   529	    return hashlib.sha256(task_id.encode()).digest()[0] % 3 == 0
   530\x09
   531\x09
   532	#: One environment for every task: the host, because the hard constraint of this
   533	#: exercise is that nothing here needs Docker. `files` cannot carry resource or timeout
   534	#: fields (`data/scaffolds/registry.py::_check_env` refuses them), so the platform's defaults
   535	#: apply: 2 cpus, 4 GB, 300 s for the harness and 300 s for the check. The CLI's
   536	#: replay-from-scratch design keeps a 30-step episode inside that.
   537	DEFAULT_ENV: dict = {"kind": "files"}
   538\x09
   539	ENV: dict[str, dict] = {r["task_id"]: {"kind": "files"} for r in _RECORDS}
   540	SETUP: dict[str, dict] = {r["task_id"]: _setup(r) for r in _RECORDS}
   541	VERIFY: dict[str, dict] = {r["task_id"]: _verifier(r) for r in _RECORDS}
   542\x09
   543	_split_mode = (os.environ.get("HG_ALFWORLD_SPLIT") or "hash").lower()
   544	if _split_mode == "none":
   545	    SPLIT = None
   546	else:
   547	    _ids = [r["task_id"] for r in _RECORDS]
   548	    SPLIT = {"train": [t for t in _ids if not _is_eval(t)],
   549	             "eval": [t for t in _ids if _is_eval(t)]}
RETURN 0

     1	"""The ALFWorld adapter's contract: what it returns, and what it refuses to leak.
     2\x09
     3	The judgement lessons live in `docs/dataset-onboarding-skill.md`; this file is the part
     4	a program can decide. The one that carries the most weight is
     5	`test_the_check_re_materializes_its_inputs`: the dataset ships `check.py` to the agent
     6	through `SETUP` so an agent can run it for feedback, and if verification used the
     7	agent's copy the agent would be grading itself. It does not, and this proves it by
     8	planting a rogue check that writes reward 1 and asserting the grade is still 0.
     9	"""
    10	from __future__ import annotations
    11\x09
    12	import copy
    13	import importlib.util

    23	# the workdir side by side. Here they live in `verify/` and `lib/`, so `lib/` is put on
    24	# the path for the one test that imports the check as a module.

    39	def _load_module(name: str, path: Path):
    40	    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(name, path)
    41	    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    42	    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    43	    return module

    46	def test_load_returns_the_benchmark_task_list(alfworld):
    47	    """134 is ALFWorld's own count for `valid_unseen` (`Evaluating with 134 games`)."""
    48	    tasks, scorable = alfworld.load()
    49	    assert len(tasks) == 134
    50	    ids = [t["task_id"] for t in tasks]
    51	    assert len(set(ids)) == len(ids)
    52	    assert all(t["goal"].strip() for t in tasks)
    53	    assert set(alfworld.VERIFY) == set(ids)
    54	    assert set(alfworld.SETUP) == set(ids)
    55	    assert set(alfworld.ENV) == set(ids)
    56	    assert set(scorable) == set(ids)

    59	def test_the_split_covers_every_task_once(alfworld):
    60	    tasks, _ = alfworld.load()
    61	    ids = {t["task_id"] for t in tasks}
    62	    train, evaluate = set(alfworld.SPLIT["train"]), set(alfworld.SPLIT["eval"])
    63	    assert train and evaluate
    64	    assert train.isdisjoint(evaluate)
    65	    assert train | evaluate == ids

    68	def test_the_goal_is_the_environment_s_own_word_for_word(alfworld):
    69	    """The goal must be the templated one TextWorld checks, not a human paraphrase.
    70\x09
    71	    ALFWorld evaluates with `goal_desc_human_anns_prob=0.0`, so the game's goal is the
    72	    templated one in the grammar. The human annotation (`turk_annotations`) says
    73	    something similar and different -- "Hold the clock and turn on the lamp." against
    74	    "examine the alarmclock with the desklamp." -- and grading against a paraphrase the
    75	    environment does not check is a task statement that lies.
    76	    """
    77	    record = alfworld._RECORDS[0]
    78	    goal = alfworld._goal(record)
    79	    assert record["goal"].startswith("Your task is to:")
    80	    assert record["goal"] in goal
    81	    assert json.loads(Path(record["game_path"]).read_text())["walkthrough"]  # sanity
    82\x09
    83\x09
    84	def test_the_reference_solution_is_stripped_from_the_agent_s_game(alfworld):
    85	    """`walkthrough` is ALFWorld's own solution to the game; the agent must not get it."""
    86	    for record in alfworld._RECORDS[:5]:
    87	        game = json.loads(record["stripped_game"])
    88	        assert "walkthrough" not in game
    89	        assert game["pddl_domain"] and game["grammar"] and game["pddl_problem"]
    90	        raw = Path(record["game_path"]).read_text()
    91	        assert "walkthrough" in raw, "the source game should have had one to strip"

   103	    # a container path and is not used here. The lint pins the `dst` rule for datasets
   104	    # that do use one.

   110	def test_the_step_budget_is_declared_once(alfworld):
   111	    """One number, read by the adapter, the CLI and the check. A second `30` drifts."""
   112	    gym_env = _load_module("alfworld_gym_env_test",

   116	    assert alfworld.STEP_BUDGET == gym_env.STEP_BUDGET == check.gym_env.STEP_BUDGET == 30
   117	    # The CLI has no budget of its own either: its default comes from `gym_env`.

   120\x09
   121\x09
   122	def test_the_check_re_materializes_its_inputs(alfworld, tmp_path):
   123	    """Plant a check that always wins; the platform's re-materialization must ignore it.
   124\x09
   125	    This is the whole reason `SETUP` and `VERIFY.inputs` both ship `check.py`: the agent
   126	    gets a copy it can run for feedback, and verification overwrites that copy before it
   127	    runs. Without the overwrite, an agent that writes `reward.txt` itself passes every
   128	    task -- a capture channel the contract would not have.
   129	    """
   130	    from eval.verification.verifier import _verify
   131\x09
   132	    record = alfworld._RECORDS[0]
   133	    tid = record["task_id"]
   134	    workdir = tmp_path / "task"
   135	    workdir.mkdir()
   136	    (workdir / alfworld.GAME_IN_WORKDIR).write_text(record["stripped_game"])
   137	    (workdir / alfworld.LOG_IN_WORKDIR).write_text("")
   138	    # The rogue copies the agent would leave behind:
   139	    (workdir / "check.py").write_text(
   140	        "import pathlib\n"
   141	        "pathlib.Path('verifier').mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n"
   142	        "pathlib.Path('verifier/reward.txt').write_text('1')\n"
   143	        "print('agent wrote a win')\n")
   144	    (workdir / "gym_env.py").write_text("raise SystemExit(1)\n")
   145\x09
   146	    # This control targets generic input re-materialization. Keep the real inputs
   147	    # and scorer-input binding, but isolate it from sealed-runtime admission;
   148	    # test_native_runtime_hp3.py / test_native_runtime_hp4.py /
   149	    # test_native_runtime_hostpin.py cover the sealed route.
   150	    spec = copy.deepcopy(alfworld.VERIFY[tid])
   151	    spec.pop("native_runtime")
   152	    verdict = _verify(workdir, spec, "", task_id=tid,
   153	                      sandbox=False, timeout_s=180, env=alfworld.ENV[tid])
   154	    assert verdict["score"] == 0.0, verdict
   155	    assert verdict["passed"] is False
   156	    assert verdict.get("evidence"), "the verdict must carry the check's evidence"
   157	    assert verdict["evidence"].get("why")
   158	    assert "no actions" in verdict["evidence"]["why"]
   159	    assert verdict["evidence"]["scorer_input"]["verified"] is True
   160	    for name in ("check.py", "gym_env.py"):
   161	        source = next(entry["from"] for entry in spec["inputs"]
   162	                      if entry["path"] == name)
   163	        assert (workdir / name).read_bytes() == Path(source).read_bytes()
   164\x09
   165\x09
   166	def test_the_sandboxed_check_can_still_write_its_verdict(alfworld, tmp_path,
   167	                                                         monkeypatch):
   168	    """The reward channel works with the namespace **on**, and only its own directory is
   169	    writable.
   170\x09
   171	    This is the test for the platform change that made a no-Docker dataset keep the
   172	    evidence channel. The first fix ran the check unsandboxed, which works but weakens
   173	    isolation: an unsandboxed check runs with the harness's workdir as `sys.path[0]`.
   174	    The version here re-opens exactly the verdict directory on top of the read-only task
   175	    tree, so `verifier/reward.txt` can be written while everything the harness produced
   176	    stays frozen.
   177	    """
   178	    from eval.containers import sandbox as sandbox_mod
   179	    from eval.verification.verifier import _verify
   180\x09
   181	    try:
   182	        sandbox_mod._bwrap()
   183	    except sandbox_mod.SandboxUnavailable:
   184	        pytest.skip("bwrap is not available on this machine")
   185\x09
   186	    record = alfworld._RECORDS[0]
   187	    tid = record["task_id"]
   188	    root = tmp_path / "work"
   189	    workdir = root / "run" / "task"
   190	    workdir.mkdir(parents=True)
   191	    (workdir / alfworld.GAME_IN_WORKDIR).write_text(record["stripped_game"])
   192	    (workdir / alfworld.LOG_IN_WORKDIR).write_text("")
   193	    (workdir / "artifact.txt").write_text("the harness's work\n")
   194	    monkeypatch.setenv("HARNESSGRAD_WORK_ROOT", str(root))
   195\x09
   196	    # Exercise the real sealed sandbox with its dataset inputs and selected task.
   197	    spec = copy.deepcopy(alfworld.VERIFY[tid])
   198	    verdict = _verify(workdir, spec, "", task_id=tid,
   199	                      sandbox=True, timeout_s=300, env=alfworld.ENV[tid])
   200	    print(json.dumps({"sandboxed_alfworld_verdict": verdict}, sort_keys=True))
   201	    assert verdict["score"] == 0.0, verdict
   202	    assert verdict["native_runtime"]["verified"] is True, verdict
   203	    assert verdict["native_runtime"]["sha256"] == spec["native_runtime"]["sha256"]
   204	    assert verdict.get("evidence"), verdict
   205	    assert verdict["evidence"]["scorer_input"]["verified"] is True
   206	    assert (workdir / alfworld.REWARD_REL).is_file()
   207	    assert (workdir / alfworld.EVIDENCE_REL).is_file()
   208	    # The one guarantee the read-only mount exists for: the check cannot edit the
   209	    # harness's artifacts, even though it can write its verdict beside them.
   210	    assert (workdir / "artifact.txt").read_text() == "the harness's work\n"
     1	"""Every published dataset must be able to show that its grade is reachable.
     2\x09
     3	This is the gate that was missing. `tests/quality/test_dataset_lint.py` checks the *contract*
     4	for every published dataset, but the two things that actually de-risk a dataset -- an oracle
     5	control that reaches 1, a do-nothing control that reaches 0, and a test that pins the official
     6	metric -- were per-dataset conventions. A new dataset could be green without them, and two
     7	that already existed (measured 2026-10-07: `tau_bench`, `terminal_bench`) had never had an
     8	oracle arm run at all.
     9\x09
    10	**Why a table and not a name-matching heuristic.** The obvious implementation -- glob
    11	`tests/quality/test_<name>_official_scorer.py` and `data/datasets/<name>/control.py` -- is wrong in
    12	both directions here, and measurably so. `test_bigbench_official_scorers.py` pins three
    13	datasets; `test_qa_dataset_contract.py` is parametrised over six; the shared QA control is
    14	`data/scaffolds/qa/control.py --dataset <name>` rather than a per-dataset script; and a refusal has no
    15	control by design. A glob would report false gaps for the datasets that are covered *better*
    16	than the convention, and the repair for a false gap is to loosen the check. So each dataset
    17	declares its own row, the coverage assertion makes the table total, and adding a dataset
    18	without a row fails the suite at the point where the author still remembers why.
    19\x09
    20	**What is run and what is not.** The by-hand arms of gates 1 and 2 run for every dataset with
    21	a control -- measured at 0.5 s (`bfcl_v3`) to 9.3 s (`alfworld`), about 40 s for all ten. The
    22	`--platform` arms are *not* run here: they multiply that by a sandboxed `run_one` each. They
    23	live in each dataset's own test file, and `tests/quality/test_new_dataset.py` runs all four
    24	arms against a generated scaffold, so the pattern itself stays proven.
    25	"""
    26	from __future__ import annotations
    27\x09

    29	import subprocess

    31	from pathlib import Path

   174\x09
   175	#: Datasets whose control is the refusal itself, so the runnable-control test skips them and
   176	#: the refusal test below applies instead.
   177	REFUSED = ("api_bank", "sotuqa", "tau2_bench", "nestful")
   178\x09
   179	#: Datasets whose platform control is *refused* rather than run, because their declared
   180	#: policy needs a trusted backend observation this checkout cannot supply: an `exec`
   181	#: task on a container, a service network, or a writable-root/interactive environment.
   182	#:
   183	#: The refusal is the correct outcome, not a failure, so these are asserted as refusals
   184	#: rather than skipped. Skipping would hide the guarantee; demanding `reward 1`/`0`
   185	#: would demand a number that claims enforcement nobody verified.
   186	#:
   187	#: **Only `gaia` belongs here, and the reason is the control's form, not the dataset's
   188	#: blockers.** This tuple is about a control that runs through `--platform` and is
   189	#: therefore stopped by the fail-closed runner hook; `gaia` is the only declared control
   190	#: that does (`data/datasets/gaia/control.py --platform`). `jericho` and `spreadsheetbench` were
   191	#: listed here while their by-hand controls were broken -- one could not start the game
   192	#: for want of a writable temp directory, the other could not submit for want of a
   193	#: module in its `SETUP`. Both were repaired, and their by-hand arms now genuinely
   194	#: measure `1` and `0`, so expecting a refusal from them would now be expecting the
   195	#: platform to hide a working measurement. Their platform-side refusal is a separate
   196	#: property and is pinned by the admission-wiring tests, not here.
   197	POLICY_REFUSED = ("gaia",)
   198\x09
   199\x09
   200	def _provisioned(name: str) -> bool:
   201	    """The dataset's own answer to "are my bytes here", as the lint asks it."""
   202	    module = registry.importlib.import_module(f"data.datasets.{name}")
   203	    status = getattr(module, "provision_status", None)
   204	    if not callable(status):
   205	        return True
   206	    try:
   207	        return bool(status().get("present"))
   208	    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - an unreadable status is not a green light
   209	        return False
   210\x09
   211\x09
   212	def test_every_published_dataset_has_a_row():
   213	    """The table must be total: a new dataset cannot be added without declaring its shape."""
   214	    published = set(registry.published())
   215	    declared = set(ROWS)
   216	    assert not published - declared, (
   217	        "published datasets with no row in tests/quality/test_dataset_controls.py: "
   218	        f"{sorted(published - declared)}. Add a row naming the control to run and the test "
   219	        "that pins the official metric -- or an exemption saying why there is none.")
   220	    assert not declared - published, (
   221	        f"rows for names that are not published datasets: {sorted(declared - published)}. "
   222	        "A stale row is a check that has stopped covering anything.")
   223\x09
   224\x09
   225	def test_the_control_and_the_metric_test_are_declared_together():
   226	    """A control with no metric test, or a metric test with no control, is half a gate."""
   227	    half = []
   228	    for name, row in ROWS.items():
   229	        has_control = bool(row["control"])
   230	        has_metric = bool(row["metric_tests"])
   231	        if has_control != has_metric and name not in REFUSED and not row.get("exempt"):
   232	            half.append(f"{name} (control={has_control}, metric_tests={has_metric})")
   233	    assert not half, (
   234	        "a dataset must declare both an oracle control and the test that pins its metric: "
   235	        + "; ".join(half))
   236\x09
   237\x09
   238	def test_exemptions_say_why_and_what_it_would_take():
   239	    """An exemption is a recorded debt, so it has to name the work, not just the absence."""
   240	    thin = [name for name, row in ROWS.items()
   241	            if not row["control"] and name not in REFUSED and not row.get("exempt")
   242	            and len(row.get("exempt", "")) < 200]
   243	    assert not thin, (
   244	        f"these datasets have no control and no real explanation: {thin}. An exemption has "
   245	        f"to say why the arm was never run and what writing one would take.")
   246\x09
   247\x09
   248	def test_every_declared_control_exists():
   249	    missing = [row["control"][0] for row in ROWS.values()
   250	               if row["control"] and not (ROOT / row["control"][0]).is_file()]
   251	    assert not missing, f"declared controls that are not there: {sorted(set(missing))}"
   252\x09
   253\x09
   254	def test_every_declared_metric_test_exists():
   255	    missing = [name for row in ROWS.values() for name in row["metric_tests"]
   256	               if not (QUALITY / name).is_file()]
   257	    assert not missing, f"declared metric tests that are not there: {sorted(set(missing))}"
   258\x09
   259\x09
   260	def test_the_refusals_are_where_the_refusal_contract_lives():
   261	    """The refusal is a first-class result, so it is declared in the file that tests it."""
   262	    source = (QUALITY / "test_dataset_refusals.py").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   263	    match = re.search(r"REFUSING\s*=\s*\[(.*?)\]", source, re.S)
   264	    assert match, "test_dataset_refusals.py no longer declares REFUSING"
   265	    listed = set(re.findall(r'"([a-z0-9_]+)"', match.group(1)))
   266	    assert listed == set(REFUSED), (
   267	        f"the two lists disagree: this file says {sorted(REFUSED)}, "
   268	        f"test_dataset_refusals.py says {sorted(listed)}")
   269\x09
   270\x09
   271	@pytest.mark.parametrize("name", sorted(n for n, r in ROWS.items() if r["control"]))
   272	def test_the_control_reaches_one_and_the_noop_reaches_zero(name):
   273	    """Gates 1 and 2, by hand. Measured 0.5-9.3 s per dataset, ~40 s for all of them."""
   274	    if not _provisioned(name):
   275	        pytest.skip(f"{name}: not provisioned on this machine")
   276	    argv = ROWS[name]["control"]
   277	    if name in POLICY_REFUSED:
   278	        # Both arms must be refused, and a refusal must never look like a score. The
   279	        # do-nothing arm is the one this guards hardest: `None` compared as `0.0` used
   280	        # to make a refused run read as "the noop arm behaved", which is a fabricated
   281	        # control result. The controls now exit 2 with CONTROL UNMEASURED.
   282	        for arm in ("--oracle", "--noop"):
   283	            proc = subprocess.run([sys.executable, *argv, arm], cwd=ROOT,
   284	                                  capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=900)
   285	            combined = proc.stdout + proc.stderr
   286	            assert proc.returncode != 0, (
   287	                f"{name} {arm} reported success for a policy the platform refuses:\n"
   288	                f"{combined[-1200:]}")
   289	            assert "UNMEASURED" in combined or "no score" in combined, (
   290	                f"{name} {arm} was refused without saying so:\n{combined[-1200:]}")
   291	            assert not re.search(r"reward\s+[01](?![\d.])", proc.stdout), (
   292	                f"{name} {arm} produced a reward for a refused task:\n{proc.stdout[-800:]}")
   293	        return
   294	    for arm, expected in (("--oracle", "1"), ("--noop", "0")):
   295	        proc = subprocess.run([sys.executable, *argv, arm], cwd=ROOT,
   296	                              capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=900)
   297	        assert proc.returncode == 0, (
   298	            f"{name} {arm} exited {proc.returncode}; a dataset whose own control fails is "
   299	            f"not gradeable:\n{proc.stdout[-1200:]}\n{proc.stderr[-600:]}")
   300	        assert re.search(rf"reward\s+{expected}(?![\d.])", proc.stdout), (
   301	            f"{name} {arm} did not report reward {expected}:\n{proc.stdout[-1200:]}")
     1	"""Acceptance contract for the completed data split (Stages A/C/D).
     2\x09
     3	Only source and filesystem metadata are read. Registry discovery receives literal
     4	KIND declarations in place of adapter imports; task pools are never opened.
     5	"""
     6	from __future__ import annotations
     7\x09
     8	import ast
     9	import importlib
    10	import importlib.util
    11	import sys

    43	def _relative(path):
    44	    return path.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix()

    46\x09
    47	def _source(path):
    48	    assert path.is_file(), f"Missing source file: {_relative(path)}"
    49	    text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    50	    return text, ast.parse(text, filename=_relative(path))
    51\x09
    52\x09
    53	def _kind(path):
    54	    """Read declarations for every adapter, including AgentDojo, without imports."""
    55	    _, tree = _source(path)
    56	    kind = "fixture"
    57	    for node in tree.body:
    58	        targets = (node.targets if isinstance(node, ast.Assign) else
    59	                   [node.target] if isinstance(node, ast.AnnAssign) else [])
    60	        if any(isinstance(target, ast.Name) and target.id == "KIND"
    61	               for target in targets):
    62	            try:
    63	                kind = ast.literal_eval(node.value)
    64	            except (ValueError, TypeError, SyntaxError):
    65	                raise AssertionError(
    66	                    f"{_relative(path)}:{node.lineno}: KIND must be literal") from None
    67	    return kind

   166	def test_adapter_imports_only_use_scaffolds_stdlib_or_third_party():
   167	    """An adapter may reach `data.scaffolds.*`; it may not reach another adapter.
   168\x09
   169	    Deliberately narrower than "any import whose root is a local name", which is what
   170	    this check first did and why it reported four false violations. Three of them were
   171	    **third-party packages that happen to share a dataset's name** -- `jericho`,
   172	    `reasoning_gym` and `tau_bench` are upstream distributions the adapters legitimately
   173	    import -- and the fourth was `eval.policy.appworld_reporting`, a `data -> eval` edge
   174	    that `tests/quality/test_import_direction.py` governs with a reviewed exception list.
   175	    A root-name rule cannot tell those apart from an adapter importing a sibling
   176	    adapter, so it tested the wrong thing.
   177\x09
   178	    What this pins is the boundary the split exists to create: no adapter imports
   179	    another adapter, and an adapter's only `data.*` dependency is `data.scaffolds.*`.
   180	    """
   181	    errors = []
   182	    for name in EXPECTED_DATASETS:
   183	        path = DATASETS / f"{name}.py"
   184	        if not path.is_file():
   185	            errors.append(f"Cannot check adapter imports: missing {_relative(path)}")
   186	            continue
   187	        text, tree = _source(path)
   188	        for node in ast.walk(tree):
   189	            if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
   190	                targets = [alias.name for alias in node.names]
   191	            elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):
   192	                base = node.module or ""
   193	                if node.level:
   194	                    if node.level > 2:
   195	                        base = "<relative import beyond data>"
   196	                    else:
   197	                        parts = ["data", "datasets"][:3 - node.level]
   198	                        base = ".".join(parts + ([base] if base else []))
   199	                targets = [f"{base}.{alias.name}" for alias in node.names]
   200	            else:
   201	                continue
   202	            for target in targets:
   203	                statement = ast.get_source_segment(text, node)
   204	                if target.startswith("data.datasets."):
   205	                    other = target.split(".")[2]
   206	                    errors.append(
   207	                        f"{_relative(path)}:{node.lineno}: {statement} "
   208	                        f"[forbidden dependency: adapter {name!r} imports adapter {other!r}]")
   209	                elif (target.startswith("data.")
   210	                      and not target.startswith(("data.scaffolds.", "data.runtimes."))):
   211	                    # `data.runtimes` is pure shared platform data (sealed-runtime pins), the same
   212	                    # category as `data.scaffolds`; adapters may import either, nothing else under data.
   213	                    errors.append(
   214	                        f"{_relative(path)}:{node.lineno}: {statement} "
   215	                        f"[forbidden dependency: {target} is not data.scaffolds.* or data.runtimes.*]")
   216	    _check(errors)
