COMMAND 6
$ sed -n '30,175p' data/datasets/alfworld/lib/gym_env.py; sed -n '1,245p' data/datasets/alfworld/verify/check.py
OBSERVED OUTPUT

The step budget
---------------
`STEP_BUDGET` is the number of `env.step` calls an episode may use. It is declared once
and used by the CLI (which refuses action 31) and by the check (which replays at most
30) and by TextWorld itself (`max_episode_steps`, which ends the episode when the
budget runs out). The three have to be the same number or a trajectory could be
legal for the agent and truncated for the check.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

#: The community/sibling-project protocol: at most 30 actions per episode. Declared
#: here rather than spelled `30` at three call sites -- see the module docstring.
STEP_BUDGET = 30

#: The file TextWorld loads. Generated by ALFWorld's own tooling and shipped in the
#: `0.4.2` release asset; it embeds the PDDL domain, the task-specific grammar and the
#: problem, so a game file is self-contained and does not need `$ALFWORLD_DATA/logic`.
GAME_FILENAME = "game.tw-pddl"

#: The agent's action log, one JSON object per line. It is the *only* thing the check
#: reads from the agent: the check never trusts an observation, a `won` flag or a
#: summary that the CLI wrote, because all of those can be forged by the agent that
#: wrote them. Actions are safe to trust in a way that verdicts are not -- replaying
#: them in a fresh environment is what turns them into a verdict.
LOG_FILENAME = "alf_actions.jsonl"


def build(game_path: str, budget: int = STEP_BUDGET):
    """A fresh, single-game TextWorld environment.

    `AlfredDemangler(shuffle=False)` is not decoration: the game file's actions use
    ALFRED's demangled object names (`desk 2`, `alarmclock 1`) and TextWorld's raw names
    are the mangled ones, so without the demangler every command is "not admissible".
    `shuffle=False` because the benchmark's evaluation is not randomised and a shuffled
    world would make two replays of one log disagree.
    """
    import textworld
    import textworld.gym
    from alfworld.agents.environment.alfred_tw_env import AlfredDemangler, AlfredInfos

    request_infos = textworld.EnvInfos(won=True, admissible_commands=True,
                                       extras=["gamefile"])
    env_id = textworld.gym.register_games(
        [str(game_path)], request_infos,
        batch_size=1,
        asynchronous=False,
        max_episode_steps=budget,
        wrappers=[AlfredDemangler(shuffle=False), AlfredInfos],
    )
    return textworld.gym.make(env_id)


def reset(env) -> dict:
    """The episode's first observation. `won` is read but is always false here."""
    obs, infos = env.reset()
    return {
        "observation": obs[0],
        "admissible": list(infos.get("admissible_commands", [[]])[0]),
        "won": bool(infos.get("won", [False])[0]),
    }


def step(env, command: str) -> dict:
    """Apply one command and return what TextWorld said, including its `won` flag."""
    obs, _scores, dones, infos = env.step([command])
    return {
        "observation": obs[0],
        "admissible": list(infos.get("admissible_commands", [[]])[0]),
        "won": bool(infos.get("won", [False])[0]),
        "done": bool(dones[0]),
    }


def replay(game_path: str, actions: list[str], budget: int = STEP_BUDGET) -> dict:
    """Replay `actions` from a fresh episode and report the environment's own verdict.

    Returns the initial observation, one record per action actually applied, and
    `won` -- true when TextWorld reported the goal satisfied at **any** step. "At any
    step" is upstream's rule, not a convenience: ALFWorld's own evaluator takes the
    maximum of the per-step `won` (`alfworld/agents/eval/evaluate_dagger.py`), and an
    episode that wins and then keeps acting is still a win.

    The replay stops early when TextWorld says `done` (a win, or the budget running
    out), which is what the official evaluator does too.
    """
    env = build(game_path, budget)
    try:
        first = reset(env)
        records: list[dict] = []
        won = first["won"]
        for action in list(actions)[:budget]:
            record = dict(step(env, action))
            record["action"] = action
            records.append(record)
            won = won or record["won"]
            if record["done"]:
                break
        return {"initial_observation": first["observation"],
                "initial_admissible": first["admissible"],
                "records": records, "won": won}
    finally:
        try:
            env.close()
        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - a closed env is not a measurement failure
            pass


def action_log_records(path) -> list[dict]:
    """Parse an action log defensively. A line this cannot read is not an action.

    Kept here rather than in the check so the CLI and the check read the log the same
    way. A malformed line is dropped rather than raising: the log is the agent's file,
    and a truncated write must not turn into a check that cannot run at all -- it turns
    into a shorter episode, which the check then scores honestly.
    """
    import json
    from pathlib import Path

    p = Path(path)
    if not p.is_file():
        return []
    out = []
    for line in p.read_text(errors="replace").splitlines():
        line = line.strip()
        if not line:
            continue
        try:
            record = json.loads(line)
        except ValueError:
            continue
        if isinstance(record, dict):
            out.append(record)
    return out
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""The ALFWorld check: replay the agent's actions in a **fresh** environment and read
the environment's own `won` flag.

This is the dataset's whole grade. It does not read a summary the agent wrote, it does
not read the agent's observation, and it does not read any `won` the agent's CLI
recorded -- all of those are the agent's own bytes and the agent can make them say
anything. It reads one thing from the agent: the ordered list of actions in
`alf_actions.jsonl`. It then rebuilds the game from a trusted copy of the game file,
applies those actions one at a time to TextWorld, and asks TextWorld whether the PDDL
goal is satisfied. Success is `infos["won"]`, exactly as
`alfworld/agents/eval/evaluate_dagger.py` reads it.

The rule, precisely
-------------------
    reward = 1  iff  the replayed episode reports `won` at some step
    reward = 0  otherwise

"At some step" is upstream's rule, not a convenience: ALFWorld's evaluator takes the
maximum of the per-step `won` scores over the episode. An episode that satisfies the
goal and then keeps acting is still a success, and the replay stops at the first `done`
(TextWorld's own end-of-episode signal) exactly as the evaluator's loop does.

**`alf done` is recorded but is not a gate.** tau-bench's check *must* require `tau
done`, because tau-bench itself only computes a reward for a finished episode. ALFWorld
has no such rule: an episode ends when TextWorld says it ends (the goal is met or the
step budget is spent), and nothing in the benchmark asks the agent to declare anything.
Adding a `done` gate here would be inventing a requirement the benchmark does not have
-- which is a different failure from dropping one, and just as misleading. The record
still carries whether `done` was declared, because it is useful evidence.

What makes this check trustworthy
---------------------------------
* The game file is re-materialized by the platform at verification time
  (`VERIFY.inputs`), so editing `<workdir>/game.tw-pddl` does not change the grade.
* The same is true of this file and of `gym_env.py`: `SETUP` ships them for the agent's
  own CLI, and verification overwrites them. The check imports `gym_env` from the
  workdir *after* that overwrite.
* An episode the check cannot measure -- a game that will not load, a game file that is
  missing -- writes **no reward file** and exits 3. The platform then reports the task
  as unmeasured rather than as a zero the agent earned. A bare 0 is indistinguishable
  from a wrong answer, and that distinction is the whole reason this file writes
  evidence.

Outputs
-------
    reward file    1 or 0   (`/verifier/reward.txt` by default)
    evidence file  JSON     why, per action: what happened, which step won, what the
                            official per-step reduction would have produced
    stdout         why, in one line, so the platform's verdict detail carries it too
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import os
import sys
import traceback
from pathlib import Path

# Resolve helpers beside trusted code, including in the sealed /scorer root.
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
from data.scaffolds.scorer_input import add_arguments, run_bound

import gym_env  # noqa: E402

#: The container contract's locations. On a host (`files`) environment these are
#: overridden by the flags below, because `/verifier` does not exist there.
DEFAULT_REWARD_FILE = "/verifier/reward.txt"
DEFAULT_EVIDENCE_FILE = "/verifier/evidence.json"


def parse_args(argv):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__.splitlines()[0])
    parser.add_argument("--workdir", default=os.environ.get("ALFWORLD_WORKDIR", "."))
    parser.add_argument("--game", default=None, help="the trusted game file")
    parser.add_argument("--log", default=None, help="the agent's action log")
    parser.add_argument("--reward-file",
                        default=os.environ.get("ALFWORLD_REWARD_FILE",
                                               DEFAULT_REWARD_FILE))
    parser.add_argument("--evidence-file",
                        default=os.environ.get("ALFWORLD_EVIDENCE_FILE",
                                               DEFAULT_EVIDENCE_FILE))
    parser.add_argument("--budget", type=int, default=gym_env.STEP_BUDGET)
    add_arguments(parser)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    workdir = Path(args.workdir)
    args.game = Path(args.game) if args.game else workdir / gym_env.GAME_FILENAME
    args.log = Path(args.log) if args.log else workdir / gym_env.LOG_FILENAME
    args.reward_file = Path(args.reward_file)
    args.evidence_file = Path(args.evidence_file)
    return args


def write_reward(path: Path, score: int) -> None:
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    path.write_text(f"{score}\n")


def write_evidence(path: Path, evidence: dict) -> None:
    """Evidence is for a reader, never for the score: failing to write it is not fatal."""
    try:
        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        path.write_text(json.dumps(evidence, indent=1, sort_keys=True,
                                   ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
    except OSError as exc:
        print(f"could not write evidence: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)


def official_reduction(won_per_step: list[bool]) -> float:
    """The per-episode score ALFWorld's own evaluator computes from a `won` sequence.

    Copied from `alfworld/agents/eval/evaluate_dagger.py`, which builds

        step_rewards = [curr - prev for curr, prev in zip(scores, prev_rewards)]
        ...
        game_points = np.max(np.array(sequence_game_points), 0).tolist()

    with `scores = [float(item) for item in infos["won"]]`. Because `won` is monotone
    (TextWorld does not un-win a game) and the sequence starts at 0.0, the maximum of
    the increments equals "did any step win", which is what this file returns. The
    reduction is computed here anyway so the evidence carries both numbers, and
    `tests/quality/test_alfworld_official_scorer.py` pins the equality against a
    verbatim copy of upstream's code -- the copy, not an import of their package.
    """
    prev = 0.0
    increments: list[float] = []
    for won in won_per_step:
        curr = 1.0 if won else 0.0
        increments.append(curr - prev)
        prev = curr
    return max(increments) if increments else 0.0


def main(argv) -> int:
    args = parse_args(argv)
    return run_bound(args, {str(args.game): args.game},
                     lambda inputs, receipt: _score(args, inputs, receipt))


def _score(args, inputs, receipt) -> int:
    evidence: dict = {
        "scorer_input": receipt,
        "game_file": str(args.game),
        "action_log": str(args.log),
        "reward_file": str(args.reward_file),
    }

    if not args.game.is_file():
        # The check's own input is missing: not a measurement.
        evidence["error"] = f"no game file at {args.game}"
        evidence["measured"] = False
        write_evidence(args.evidence_file, evidence)
        print(f"check cannot run: {evidence['error']}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 3

    records = gym_env.action_log_records(args.log)
    actions = [str(r["action"]) for r in records
               if r.get("kind") == "action" and isinstance(r.get("action"), str)]
    declared_done = any(r.get("kind") == "done" for r in records)
    unparseable = len([line for line in
                       (args.log.read_text(errors="replace").splitlines()
                        if args.log.is_file() else []) if line.strip()]) - len(records)
    evidence.update(actions_in_log=len(actions), log_records=len(records),
                    unparseable_lines=unparseable, agent_declared_done=declared_done,
                    budget=args.budget)

    try:
        state = gym_env.replay(str(inputs[str(args.game)]), actions, args.budget)
    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a check that cannot run is not a zero
        evidence["error"] = f"could not build or replay the environment: {exc}"
        evidence["traceback"] = traceback.format_exc()[-2000:]
        evidence["measured"] = False
        write_evidence(args.evidence_file, evidence)
        print(f"check cannot run: {evidence['error']}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 3

    won_per_step = [bool(r["won"]) for r in state["records"]]
    won_step = next((i for i, w in enumerate(won_per_step) if w), None)
    replayed = state["records"]
    budget_exhausted = (len(actions) > len(replayed)
                        and not (replayed and replayed[-1]["done"]))
    # A command TextWorld did not understand comes back as "Nothing happens." It still
    # cost a step, and recording it is how a reader tells "the agent tried and was
    # refused" apart from "the agent did nothing".
    no_ops = [r["action"] for r in replayed
              if "Nothing happens" in str(r.get("observation", ""))]
    reward = 1 if state["won"] else 0
    official = official_reduction(won_per_step)

    evidence.update(
        measured=True,
        reward=reward,
        won=bool(state["won"]),
        won_at_step=won_step,
        actions_replayed=len(replayed),
        actions_not_replayed=len(actions) - len(replayed),
        budget_exhausted=bool(budget_exhausted),
        episode_done=bool(replayed and replayed[-1]["done"]),
        no_op_actions=no_ops,
        won_per_step=won_per_step,
        official_game_points=official,
        # The agent's own narrative is not trusted, so it is not included. What is
        # included is the *action* at each step and TextWorld's answer, which is the
        # material a reader needs to see why the reward is what it is.
        steps=[{"step": i, "action": r["action"], "won": bool(r["won"]),
                "done": bool(r["done"]),
                "observation": str(r.get("observation", ""))[:200]}
               for i, r in enumerate(replayed)],
    )

    if won_step is None:
        if budget_exhausted:
            why = (f"the goal was not satisfied in the {args.budget}-step budget; the "
                   f"log holds {len(actions)} actions and only {len(replayed)} replayed")
        elif not actions:
            why = "the log holds no actions, so the goal cannot have been satisfied"
        else:
            why = (f"the goal was not satisfied after {len(replayed)} action(s); the "
                   f"episode ended without a win")
    else:
        why = f"the goal was satisfied at step {won_step + 1} of {len(replayed)}"
    evidence["why"] = why
    write_evidence(args.evidence_file, evidence)

    if reward == 1:
        write_reward(args.reward_file, 1)
        print(f"reward 1: {why} (official game points {official:g}; "
              f"agent declared done={declared_done})")
        return 0

    write_reward(args.reward_file, 0)
    print(f"reward 0: {why} (official game points {official:g}; "
          f"no-op actions {len(no_ops)})")
    return 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    try:
        raise SystemExit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
    except SystemExit:
        raise
    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - a crash must not look like a measured zero
        traceback.print_exc()
        raise SystemExit(4)
RETURN 0

#:
#: `None` is the accurate value only because nothing in the pool is study material -- it does
#: not claim the pool is the whole of the benchmark's evaluation set (see `ds1000`, which is a
#: measured subset). `--split official --side eval` is the comparable run: the whole pool, with
#: no study material in it. `--split declared` keeps this adapter's own division.
OFFICIAL_EVAL = None

import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
from pathlib import Path

#: Where the data lives. Outside the platform tree on purpose: dataset content is not
#: platform code, is never committed, and is what `tools/datasets.py` provisions.
#: `HG_ALFWORLD_DATA` overrides it so a reader can point at another copy.
DATASET_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("HG_ALFWORLD_DATA",
                                   "/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/datasets/alfworld"))

#: The split this dataset is. `valid_seen` and `train` are on disk too; only the
#: out-of-distribution evaluation split is used, which is the one ALFWorld reports.
DATA_SUBDIR = ("json_2.1.1", "valid_unseen")

#: This module's own directory: the adapter is `data/datasets/alfworld.py`, its content is
#: `data/datasets/alfworld/`.
ASSETS = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "alfworld"
CLI_DIR = ASSETS / "cli"
LIB_DIR = ASSETS / "lib"
VERIFY_DIR = ASSETS / "verify"

#: The step budget and the two filenames are **declared once**, in
#: `data/datasets/alfworld/lib/gym_env.py`, which the agent's CLI and the check both import.
#: Repeating `30` here would be a second declaration that can drift from the one the
#: environment is built with -- and a drift there means an action is legal for the agent
#: and truncated for the check. Loaded by file path rather than by `import gym_env`,
#: because `lib/` is not on `sys.path` and putting it there from inside a dataset module
#: would leak a generic module name into the driver's interpreter.
def _load_gym_env():
    import importlib.util
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("alfworld_gym_env",
                                                  LIB_DIR / "gym_env.py")
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


_GYM_ENV = _load_gym_env()

#: The community/sibling-project protocol: at most 30 actions per episode.
STEP_BUDGET = _GYM_ENV.STEP_BUDGET
GAME_IN_WORKDIR = _GYM_ENV.GAME_FILENAME
LOG_IN_WORKDIR = _GYM_ENV.LOG_FILENAME

#: Where the check is told to put its verdict. The absolute paths are the container
#: contract; on a `files` environment the platform resolves them against the workdir
#: (`eval/verification/verifier.py::_verify`), and the check's `argv` passes the same location in the
#: relative spelling its own working directory understands.
REWARD_FILE = "/verifier/reward.txt"
EVIDENCE_FILE = "/verifier/evidence.json"
REWARD_REL = "verifier/reward.txt"
EVIDENCE_REL = "verifier/evidence.json"

#: ALFWorld's task-type vocabulary. A `traj_data.json` whose `task_type` is not in this
#: set is not a task ALFWorld evaluates, so it is not one this dataset returns.
TASK_TYPES = {
    1: "pick_and_place_simple",
    2: "look_at_obj_in_light",
    3: "pick_clean_then_place_in_recep",
    4: "pick_heat_then_place_in_recep",
    5: "pick_cool_then_place_in_recep",
    6: "pick_two_obj_and_place",
}

#: The templated goal lives in the game's TextWorld grammar, as the `rhs` of the `task`
#: rule. The grammar is TWL2 (JSON with `#` comments), so it cannot be `json.loads`-ed;
#: the rule is pulled out with a regex and unescaped as a JSON string. Verified on all
#: 134 valid_unseen games.
_GOAL_RE = re.compile(r'"task"\s*:\s*\[\s*\{\s*"rhs"\s*:\s*"((?:[^"\\]|\\.)*)"')

#: The one field of a game file that must never reach the agent. `walkthrough` is
#: ALFWorld's own reference solution for the game -- a list of the exact commands that
#: solve it. The game file is the task's initial state, so it is handed to the agent;
#: handing over the walkthrough would hand over the answer. `SETUP` and every verifier
#: input therefore carry `_stripped_game()` rather than the file on disk, and the
#: oracle control reads the original from the data root.
_LEAKED_FIELD = "walkthrough"


def _split_dir() -> Path:
    return DATASET_ROOT.joinpath(*DATA_SUBDIR)


def _collect() -> list[dict]:
    """Every runnable game in the split, filtered exactly as ALFWorld filters them.

    The filter is `AlfredTWEnv.collect_game_files`, reproduced rather than called:
    calling it would drag in `textworld` and crawl the whole data tree at import time,
    and the adapter must be importable on a machine that has not provisioned the data
    (see `published()` in `data/scaffolds/registry.py`: a module that will not import is hidden
    from the chooser). The rules, in the same order upstream applies them:

      * a trial directory must hold `traj_data.json` and `game.tw-pddl`;
      * its path must not contain `movable` or `Sliced` (unsupported trajectories);
      * `traj_data.json`'s `task_type` must be one ALFWorld evaluates;
      * `game.tw-pddl` must carry `solvable` and it must be true.

    Each returned record is everything the adapter needs and nothing it does not.
    """
    root = _split_dir()
    records: list[dict] = []
    for game_path in sorted(root.glob("*/*/game.tw-pddl")):
        trial_dir = game_path.parent
        task_dir = trial_dir.parent
        label = f"{task_dir.name}/{trial_dir.name}"
        if "movable" in str(trial_dir) or "Sliced" in str(trial_dir):
            continue
        traj_path = trial_dir / "traj_data.json"
        if not traj_path.is_file():
            continue
        try:
            traj_data = json.loads(traj_path.read_text())
            gamedata = json.loads(game_path.read_text())
        except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
            raise ValueError(f"alfworld: {label} is unreadable: {exc}") from exc
        if traj_data.get("task_type") not in TASK_TYPES.values():
            continue
        if "solvable" not in gamedata:
            # The 0.4.2 asset is the only one with this key; an extraction that skipped
            # it lands here for every trial. Say that rather than "0 tasks".
            raise ValueError(
                f"alfworld: {label} has no `solvable` key -- the game files are not the "
                f"0.4.2 `json_2.1.3_tw-pddl.zip` ones. See downloads() and "
                f"tools/datasets.py --check")
        if not gamedata["solvable"]:
            continue
        match = _GOAL_RE.search(str(gamedata.get("grammar") or ""))
        if not match:
            raise ValueError(
                f"alfworld: {label}: the game grammar has no `task` rule, so the "
                f"goal text cannot be read")
        goal = json.loads('"' + match.group(1) + '"')
        records.append({
            # The official per-game label is the last two path components
            # (`evaluate_dagger` prints `"/".join(game_names[i].split("/")[-3:-1])`).
            # A slash is not safe in a platform task id (it becomes a container name
            # and a path component), so it is spelled `__` here.
                    "on its own it cannot make a task.",
        },
        {
            "kind": "http",
            "name": "json_2.1.1_pddl.zip",
            "version": "0.2.2",
            "url": "https://github.com/alfworld/alfworld/releases/download/"
                   "0.2.2/json_2.1.1_pddl.zip",
            "size": "~35 MB",
            "sha256": "913942ebed06659ea0da2f8122512d98bc6add30d84961ca803132d8fbcad585",
            "dst": "_src/json_2.1.1_pddl.zip",
            "extract_to": ".",
            "note": "Holds only `initial_state.pddl` (7080 files). Measured: the loading "
                    "path does not read it (the 0.4.2 game file embeds `pddl_problem`), "
                    "but ALFWorld's generation path does, and `alfworld-download` "
                    "fetches it, so it is provisioned.",
        },
        {
            "kind": "http",
            "name": "json_2.1.3_tw-pddl.zip",
            "version": "0.4.2",
            "url": "https://github.com/alfworld/alfworld/releases/download/"
                   "0.4.2/json_2.1.3_tw-pddl.zip",
            "size": "~35 MB",
            "sha256": "5df77ea759f2211a4106082839ddbbb790f1ba4e7d097ed732cf453f72aa36cf",
            "dst": "_src/json_2.1.3_tw-pddl.zip",
            "extract_to": ".",
            "note": "Trap 2: the ONLY archive with `game.tw-pddl`, and therefore the "
                    "only one with the `solvable` key (and the `walkthrough` we strip). "
                    "A version other than 0.4.2 has no `solvable` and every task is "
                    "filtered out.",
        },
        {
            "kind": "http",
            "name": "alfworld repo tarball",
            "version": "aaba6870f86c5be6a08a491f32a50b906227bc3e",
            "url": "https://github.com/alfworld/alfworld/archive/"
                   "aaba6870f86c5be6a08a491f32a50b906227bc3e.tar.gz",
            "size": "~3.8 MB",
            "sha256": "5592fbb36124b08d24167c5f7612a55a2cc610e0c39170f638a69b628835ee3b",

            "note": "Trap 3: the PDDL domain and the TextWorld grammar live here, at "
                    "`alfworld/data/alfred.pddl` and `alfworld/data/alfred.twl2` -- not "

                    "`$ALFWORLD_DATA/logic/`. Pinned by commit sha, not by `main`.",
        },

        },
    ]


def provision_status() -> dict:
    """What is present locally, for `tools/datasets.py --check`.

    Deliberately does not import `textworld` or build an environment: this is the
    answer to "did the provisioning run", which has to work on a machine where it did
    not.
    """
    split = _split_dir()
    games = sorted(split.glob("*/*/game.tw-pddl")) if split.is_dir() else []
    solvable = 0
    for game in games:
        try:
            if "solvable" in json.loads(game.read_text()):
                solvable += 1
        except (OSError, ValueError):
            continue
    logic = DATASET_ROOT / "logic"
    return {
        "root": str(DATASET_ROOT),
        "present": bool(games),
        "split_dir": str(split),
        "game_files": len(games),
        "with_solvable": solvable,
        "logic_files": sorted(p.name for p in logic.glob("*")) if logic.is_dir() else [],
        "tasks": len(_RECORDS),
    }

COMMAND 8
$ sed -n '1,265p' tests/quality/test_alfworld_dataset.py
OBSERVED OUTPUT
"""The ALFWorld adapter's contract: what it returns, and what it refuses to leak.

The judgement lessons live in `docs/dataset-onboarding-skill.md`; this file is the part
a program can decide. The one that carries the most weight is
`test_the_check_re_materializes_its_inputs`: the dataset ships `check.py` to the agent
through `SETUP` so an agent can run it for feedback, and if verification used the
agent's copy the agent would be grading itself. It does not, and this proves it by
planting a rogue check that writes reward 1 and asserting the grade is still 0.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import copy
import importlib.util
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT))
# The check imports `gym_env` from its own directory; in a real run they are copied into
# the workdir side by side. Here they live in `verify/` and `lib/`, so `lib/` is put on
# the path for the one test that imports the check as a module.
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "alfworld" / "lib"))

pytest.importorskip("textworld", reason="ALFWorld needs textworld")
pytest.importorskip("alfworld", reason="ALFWorld needs the alfworld package")


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def alfworld():
    import data.datasets.alfworld as module
    if not module._RECORDS:
        pytest.skip(f"no extracted ALFWorld data under {module.DATASET_ROOT}")
    return module


def _load_module(name: str, path: Path):
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(name, path)
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


def test_load_returns_the_benchmark_task_list(alfworld):
    """134 is ALFWorld's own count for `valid_unseen` (`Evaluating with 134 games`)."""
    tasks, scorable = alfworld.load()
    assert len(tasks) == 134
    ids = [t["task_id"] for t in tasks]
    assert len(set(ids)) == len(ids)
    assert all(t["goal"].strip() for t in tasks)
    assert set(alfworld.VERIFY) == set(ids)
    assert set(alfworld.SETUP) == set(ids)
    assert set(alfworld.ENV) == set(ids)
    assert set(scorable) == set(ids)


def test_the_split_covers_every_task_once(alfworld):
    tasks, _ = alfworld.load()
    ids = {t["task_id"] for t in tasks}
    train, evaluate = set(alfworld.SPLIT["train"]), set(alfworld.SPLIT["eval"])
    assert train and evaluate
    assert train.isdisjoint(evaluate)
    assert train | evaluate == ids


def test_the_goal_is_the_environment_s_own_word_for_word(alfworld):
    """The goal must be the templated one TextWorld checks, not a human paraphrase.

    ALFWorld evaluates with `goal_desc_human_anns_prob=0.0`, so the game's goal is the
    templated one in the grammar. The human annotation (`turk_annotations`) says
    something similar and different -- "Hold the clock and turn on the lamp." against
    "examine the alarmclock with the desklamp." -- and grading against a paraphrase the
    environment does not check is a task statement that lies.
    """
    record = alfworld._RECORDS[0]
    goal = alfworld._goal(record)
    assert record["goal"].startswith("Your task is to:")
    assert record["goal"] in goal
    assert json.loads(Path(record["game_path"]).read_text())["walkthrough"]  # sanity


def test_the_reference_solution_is_stripped_from_the_agent_s_game(alfworld):
    """`walkthrough` is ALFWorld's own solution to the game; the agent must not get it."""
    for record in alfworld._RECORDS[:5]:
        game = json.loads(record["stripped_game"])
        assert "walkthrough" not in game
        assert game["pddl_domain"] and game["grammar"] and game["pddl_problem"]
        raw = Path(record["game_path"]).read_text()
        assert "walkthrough" in raw, "the source game should have had one to strip"


def test_the_verifier_declares_the_contract_paths(alfworld):
    tid = alfworld._RECORDS[0]["task_id"]
    verifier = alfworld.VERIFY[tid]
    assert verifier["kind"] == "command"
    assert verifier["reward_file"] == alfworld.REWARD_FILE
    assert verifier["reward_file"].startswith("/")
    assert verifier["evidence_file"].startswith("/")
    assert verifier["timeout_s"] > 0
    # A host (`files`) environment materializes `path` inputs into the workdir; `dst` is
    # a container path and is not used here. The lint pins the `dst` rule for datasets
    # that do use one.
    assert all("path" in entry for entry in verifier["inputs"])
    assert any(entry["path"] == "check.py" for entry in verifier["inputs"])
    assert any(entry["path"] == alfworld.GAME_IN_WORKDIR for entry in verifier["inputs"])


def test_the_step_budget_is_declared_once(alfworld):
    """One number, read by the adapter, the CLI and the check. A second `30` drifts."""
    gym_env = _load_module("alfworld_gym_env_test",
                           ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "alfworld" / "lib" / "gym_env.py")
    check = _load_module("alfworld_check_budget_test",
                         ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "alfworld" / "verify" / "check.py")
    assert alfworld.STEP_BUDGET == gym_env.STEP_BUDGET == check.gym_env.STEP_BUDGET == 30
    # The CLI has no budget of its own either: its default comes from `gym_env`.
    cli = (ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "alfworld" / "cli" / "alf").read_text()
    assert "gym_env.STEP_BUDGET" in cli


def test_the_check_re_materializes_its_inputs(alfworld, tmp_path):
    """Plant a check that always wins; the platform's re-materialization must ignore it.

    This is the whole reason `SETUP` and `VERIFY.inputs` both ship `check.py`: the agent
    gets a copy it can run for feedback, and verification overwrites that copy before it
    runs. Without the overwrite, an agent that writes `reward.txt` itself passes every
    task -- a capture channel the contract would not have.
    """
    from eval.verification.verifier import _verify

    record = alfworld._RECORDS[0]
    tid = record["task_id"]
    workdir = tmp_path / "task"
    workdir.mkdir()
    (workdir / alfworld.GAME_IN_WORKDIR).write_text(record["stripped_game"])
    (workdir / alfworld.LOG_IN_WORKDIR).write_text("")
    # The rogue copies the agent would leave behind:
    (workdir / "check.py").write_text(
        "import pathlib\n"
        "pathlib.Path('verifier').mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n"
        "pathlib.Path('verifier/reward.txt').write_text('1')\n"
        "print('agent wrote a win')\n")
    (workdir / "gym_env.py").write_text("raise SystemExit(1)\n")

    # This control targets generic input re-materialization. Keep the real inputs
    # and scorer-input binding, but isolate it from sealed-runtime admission;
    # test_native_runtime_hp3.py / test_native_runtime_hp4.py /
    # test_native_runtime_hostpin.py cover the sealed route.
    spec = copy.deepcopy(alfworld.VERIFY[tid])
    spec.pop("native_runtime")
    verdict = _verify(workdir, spec, "", task_id=tid,
                      sandbox=False, timeout_s=180, env=alfworld.ENV[tid])
    assert verdict["score"] == 0.0, verdict
    assert verdict["passed"] is False
    assert verdict.get("evidence"), "the verdict must carry the check's evidence"
    assert verdict["evidence"].get("why")
    assert "no actions" in verdict["evidence"]["why"]
    assert verdict["evidence"]["scorer_input"]["verified"] is True
    for name in ("check.py", "gym_env.py"):
        source = next(entry["from"] for entry in spec["inputs"]
                      if entry["path"] == name)
        assert (workdir / name).read_bytes() == Path(source).read_bytes()


def test_the_sandboxed_check_can_still_write_its_verdict(alfworld, tmp_path,
                                                         monkeypatch):
    """The reward channel works with the namespace **on**, and only its own directory is
    writable.

    This is the test for the platform change that made a no-Docker dataset keep the
    evidence channel. The first fix ran the check unsandboxed, which works but weakens
    isolation: an unsandboxed check runs with the harness's workdir as `sys.path[0]`.
    The version here re-opens exactly the verdict directory on top of the read-only task
    tree, so `verifier/reward.txt` can be written while everything the harness produced
    stays frozen.
    """
    from eval.containers import sandbox as sandbox_mod
    from eval.verification.verifier import _verify

    try:
        sandbox_mod._bwrap()
    except sandbox_mod.SandboxUnavailable:
        pytest.skip("bwrap is not available on this machine")

    record = alfworld._RECORDS[0]
    tid = record["task_id"]
    root = tmp_path / "work"
    workdir = root / "run" / "task"
    workdir.mkdir(parents=True)
    (workdir / alfworld.GAME_IN_WORKDIR).write_text(record["stripped_game"])
    (workdir / alfworld.LOG_IN_WORKDIR).write_text("")
    (workdir / "artifact.txt").write_text("the harness's work\n")
    monkeypatch.setenv("HARNESSGRAD_WORK_ROOT", str(root))

    # Exercise the real sealed sandbox with its dataset inputs and selected task.
    spec = copy.deepcopy(alfworld.VERIFY[tid])
    verdict = _verify(workdir, spec, "", task_id=tid,
                      sandbox=True, timeout_s=300, env=alfworld.ENV[tid])
    print(json.dumps({"sandboxed_alfworld_verdict": verdict}, sort_keys=True))
    assert verdict["score"] == 0.0, verdict
    assert verdict["native_runtime"]["verified"] is True, verdict
    assert verdict["native_runtime"]["sha256"] == spec["native_runtime"]["sha256"]
    assert verdict.get("evidence"), verdict
    assert verdict["evidence"]["scorer_input"]["verified"] is True
    assert (workdir / alfworld.REWARD_REL).is_file()
    assert (workdir / alfworld.EVIDENCE_REL).is_file()
    # The one guarantee the read-only mount exists for: the check cannot edit the
    # harness's artifacts, even though it can write its verdict beside them.
    assert (workdir / "artifact.txt").read_text() == "the harness's work\n"
RETURN 0

OBSERVED OUTPUT
"""ALFWorld's own scorer, copied in, and a differential test against our check.

The rule this file exists to enforce
------------------------------------
Our reward must be **the paper's metric**, and it must be possible to see that without
trusting us. So the reduction ALFWorld's evaluator performs is transcribed here, and our
check's reward is compared with it on real games. The transcription is not an import of
`alfworld`: importing their package and calling our own function "the same" would prove
only that we can call their code, not that our reward is their metric. The copied lines
are quoted with their upstream line numbers, and a drift test below asserts they are
still verbatim in the upstream file when that checkout is present.

Why "any step won" and not "the last step won"
----------------------------------------------
Upstream builds a per-step reward as `score[t] - score[t-1]` and takes
`np.max(sequence_game_points, axis=0)` -- the maximum increment, not the final value.
An episode that satisfies the goal and then keeps acting is still a success. Our check
returns `1 iff won at some step`, which is the same predicate; the differential test
below pins the equality rather than asserting it in prose.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import importlib.util
import json
import os
import sys
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pytest

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT))
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "alfworld" / "lib"))

DATA_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("HG_ALFWORLD_DATA",
                                "/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/datasets/alfworld"))
UPSTREAM = (DATA_ROOT / "_src" / "repo" / "alfworld" / "agents" / "eval"
            / "evaluate_dagger.py")
LIB = ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "alfworld" / "lib"
CHECK = ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "alfworld" / "verify" / "check.py"

pytest.importorskip("textworld", reason="ALFWorld needs textworld")
pytest.importorskip("alfworld", reason="ALFWorld needs the alfworld package")


def official_game_points(won_per_step) -> float:
    """**Upstream's reduction, transcribed for a batch of one.**

    From `alfworld/agents/eval/evaluate_dagger.py`; the line numbers are upstream's:

        98   scores = [float(item) for item in infos["won"]]
       122   step_rewards = [float(curr) - float(prev) for curr, prev in zip(scores, prev_rewards)]
       123   prev_rewards = scores
       124   sequence_game_points.append(step_rewards)
       134   game_points = np.max(np.array(sequence_game_points), 0).tolist()

    Upstream keeps `prev_rewards` as the batch's previous scores and zips; with one game
    that is a one-element list, which is the only change made here. Nothing else is
    adapted: the arithmetic, the `np.max(..., 0)` axis and the `.tolist()` are upstream's.
    `test_the_copied_lines_are_still_verbatim_upstream` fails if they ever stop matching.
    """
    prev_rewards = [0.0]
    sequence_game_points = []
    scores = [float(item) for item in won_per_step]
    for curr in scores:
        step_rewards = [float(curr) - float(prev)
                        for curr, prev in zip([curr], prev_rewards)]
        prev_rewards = [curr]
        sequence_game_points.append(step_rewards)
    game_points = np.max(np.array(sequence_game_points), 0).tolist()
    return float(game_points[0])


def _check_module():
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("alfworld_check", CHECK)
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


def _data_available() -> bool:
    try:
        import data.datasets.alfworld as alfworld  # noqa: F401
        return bool(alfworld._RECORDS)
    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001
        return False


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def alfworld():
    if not _data_available():
        pytest.skip(f"no extracted ALFWorld data under {DATA_ROOT}")
    import data.datasets.alfworld as module
    return module


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def control():
    path = ROOT / "data" / "datasets" / "alfworld" / "control.py"
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("alfworld_control", path)
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


def _record(task_type: str):
    import data.datasets.alfworld as alfworld
    wanted = [r for r in alfworld._RECORDS if r["task_type"] == task_type]
    assert wanted, f"no task of type {task_type}"
    # Shortest walkthrough of the type, so the differential test stays cheap while still
    # exercising a real multi-step episode.
    return min(wanted, key=lambda r: len(json.loads(
        Path(r["game_path"]).read_text()).get("walkthrough") or []))


def _walkthrough(record) -> list[str]:
    return list(json.loads(Path(record["game_path"]).read_text())["walkthrough"])



The split
---------
The benchmark's own evaluation split is *all* of `valid_unseen` and it declares no
train/eval division. The platform needs one, so it is **imposed here**, derived from the
task id by hash so that adding or removing a task does not reshuffle the other side
(`_is_eval`, the same rule `data/datasets/terminal_bench.py` uses). A score on the eval side is
a score on about a third of `valid_unseen`, not on the benchmark; `HG_ALFWORLD_SPLIT=none`
runs every task as eval and accepts the driver's warning.

Noise, before anyone compares two ALFWorld runs
-----------------------------------------------
Measured on this machine with `qwen3.5-9b-local`, the same arm, the same 20 tasks, the
same seed and temperature 0, over five batches: **10 / 8 / 11 / 11 / 11 tasks, SD 1.30
tasks = 6.5 percentage points, 4 of 20 tasks changed side at least once.** A move smaller
than roughly 6 points on 20 tasks is the noise. The full note is in
`docs/experiment_noise.md`; quote the task count with every ALFWorld number, because the
granularity is `1/n`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from data.scaffolds.scorer_input import bind as bind_scorer_input

#: See `data/scaffolds/registry.py:KIND_DATASET`. A fixture is platform test material and is
#: deliberately not offered as a choice in the Launch form.
            f"trial without `game.tw-pddl`. See downloads() and "
            f"`python3 tools/datasets.py --check`.")
    tasks = [{"task_id": r["task_id"], "goal": _goal(r), "inputs": []}
             for r in _RECORDS]
    return tasks, {r["task_id"]: "" for r in _RECORDS}


def _is_eval(task_id: str) -> bool:
    """A deterministic third, by hash of the task id.

    Hashed rather than sliced off the sorted list so that adding or removing a task
    does not reshuffle which side every other task is on -- a split that moves under
    the benchmark is a split whose past scores cannot be compared with its future ones.
    """
    return hashlib.sha256(task_id.encode()).digest()[0] % 3 == 0


#: One environment for every task: the host, because the hard constraint of this
#: exercise is that nothing here needs Docker. `files` cannot carry resource or timeout
#: fields (`data/scaffolds/registry.py::_check_env` refuses them), so the platform's defaults
#: apply: 2 cpus, 4 GB, 300 s for the harness and 300 s for the check. The CLI's
#: replay-from-scratch design keeps a 30-step episode inside that.
DEFAULT_ENV: dict = {"kind": "files"}

ENV: dict[str, dict] = {r["task_id"]: {"kind": "files"} for r in _RECORDS}

#: deliberately not offered as a choice in the Launch form.
KIND = "dataset"

#: The oracle control submits the benchmark's own released reference; see
#: `data/dataset.schema.json` (`module.oracle_about`) for what the other value means.
ORACLE_SOURCE = "released"


#: The benchmark's own evaluation set, within this adapter's pool: **every task here belongs
#: to it**. The pool is `valid_unseen`, which is exactly ALFWorld's own evaluation split (134 tasks). The benchmark's 3,553-task `train` set is not loaded, so this pool holds no study material.
#:
#: `None` is the accurate value only because nothing in the pool is study material -- it does
#: not claim the pool is the whole of the benchmark's evaluation set (see `ds1000`, which is a
#: measured subset). `--split official --side eval` is the comparable run: the whole pool, with
#: no study material in it. `--split declared` keeps this adapter's own division.
OFFICIAL_EVAL = None

import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
from pathlib import Path

#: Where the data lives. Outside the platform tree on purpose: dataset content is not
#: platform code, is never committed, and is what `tools/datasets.py` provisions.
#: `HG_ALFWORLD_DATA` overrides it so a reader can point at another copy.
DATASET_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("HG_ALFWORLD_DATA",
                                   "/mnt/20t/xubuqiang/datasets/alfworld"))

#: The split this dataset is. `valid_seen` and `train` are on disk too; only the
#: out-of-distribution evaluation split is used, which is the one ALFWorld reports.
DATA_SUBDIR = ("json_2.1.1", "valid_unseen")

#: This module's own directory: the adapter is `data/datasets/alfworld.py`, its content is
#: `data/datasets/alfworld/`.
ASSETS = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "alfworld"
CLI_DIR = ASSETS / "cli"
LIB_DIR = ASSETS / "lib"
VERIFY_DIR = ASSETS / "verify"

#: The step budget and the two filenames are **declared once**, in
#: `data/datasets/alfworld/lib/gym_env.py`, which the agent's CLI and the check both import.
#: Repeating `30` here would be a second declaration that can drift from the one the
#: environment is built with -- and a drift there means an action is legal for the agent
#: and truncated for the check. Loaded by file path rather than by `import gym_env`,
#: because `lib/` is not on `sys.path` and putting it there from inside a dataset module
#: would leak a generic module name into the driver's interpreter.
def _load_gym_env():
    import importlib.util
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("alfworld_gym_env",
                                                  LIB_DIR / "gym_env.py")
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


_GYM_ENV = _load_gym_env()

#: The community/sibling-project protocol: at most 30 actions per episode.
STEP_BUDGET = _GYM_ENV.STEP_BUDGET
GAME_IN_WORKDIR = _GYM_ENV.GAME_FILENAME
LOG_IN_WORKDIR = _GYM_ENV.LOG_FILENAME

#: Where the check is told to put its verdict. The absolute paths are the container
#: contract; on a `files` environment the platform resolves them against the workdir
#: (`eval/verification/verifier.py::_verify`), and the check's `argv` passes the same location in the
#: relative spelling its own working directory understands.
REWARD_FILE = "/verifier/reward.txt"
EVIDENCE_FILE = "/verifier/evidence.json"
RETURN 0

#: the rule is pulled out with a regex and unescaped as a JSON string. Verified on all
#: 134 valid_unseen games.
_GOAL_RE = re.compile(r'"task"\s*:\s*\[\s*\{\s*"rhs"\s*:\s*"((?:[^"\\]|\\.)*)"')

#: The one field of a game file that must never reach the agent. `walkthrough` is
#: ALFWorld's own reference solution for the game -- a list of the exact commands that
#: solve it. The game file is the task's initial state, so it is handed to the agent;
#: handing over the walkthrough would hand over the answer. `SETUP` and every verifier
#: input therefore carry `_stripped_game()` rather than the file on disk, and the
#: oracle control reads the original from the data root.
_LEAKED_FIELD = "walkthrough"


def _split_dir() -> Path:
    return DATASET_ROOT.joinpath(*DATA_SUBDIR)


def _collect() -> list[dict]:
    """Every runnable game in the split, filtered exactly as ALFWorld filters them.

    The filter is `AlfredTWEnv.collect_game_files`, reproduced rather than called:
    calling it would drag in `textworld` and crawl the whole data tree at import time,
    and the adapter must be importable on a machine that has not provisioned the data
    (see `published()` in `data/scaffolds/registry.py`: a module that will not import is hidden
    from the chooser). The rules, in the same order upstream applies them:

      * a trial directory must hold `traj_data.json` and `game.tw-pddl`;
      * its path must not contain `movable` or `Sliced` (unsupported trajectories);
      * `traj_data.json`'s `task_type` must be one ALFWorld evaluates;
      * `game.tw-pddl` must carry `solvable` and it must be true.

    Each returned record is everything the adapter needs and nothing it does not.
    """
    root = _split_dir()
    records: list[dict] = []
    for game_path in sorted(root.glob("*/*/game.tw-pddl")):
        trial_dir = game_path.parent
        task_dir = trial_dir.parent
        label = f"{task_dir.name}/{trial_dir.name}"
        if "movable" in str(trial_dir) or "Sliced" in str(trial_dir):
            continue
        traj_path = trial_dir / "traj_data.json"
        if not traj_path.is_file():
            continue
        try:
            traj_data = json.loads(traj_path.read_text())
            gamedata = json.loads(game_path.read_text())
        except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
            raise ValueError(f"alfworld: {label} is unreadable: {exc}") from exc
        if traj_data.get("task_type") not in TASK_TYPES.values():
            continue
        if "solvable" not in gamedata:
            # The 0.4.2 asset is the only one with this key; an extraction that skipped
            # it lands here for every trial. Say that rather than "0 tasks".
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Validate the source-only blocker index; never import adapters or open receipts."""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import ast
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
REGISTRY = ROOT / "registry/dataset-blockers.json"
HISTORY = ROOT / "tests/fixtures/dataset_blockers/history-codex178.json"
# Pin the review baseline independently of the editable registry.
HISTORY_SHA256 = "4c778d435f4147eb04dbcee4e37a2bac3f0ac5a55584e3adf252ab12972d1300"
STATUSES = {"code-solvable", "external", "runtime", "measurement"}
FAMILIES = {
    "selected-only-privacy", "source-provenance", "scorer-dependency-network",
    "trusted-verifier-runtime", "artifact-only-export", "filesystem-quota",
    "official-scorer-execution", "snapshot-cleanup", "measurement-coverage",
    "metric-comparability", "platform-integrity", "actor-protocol",
    "termination-audit", "contract-refusal", "journal-coverage", "documentation-conflict",
}


class RegistryError(ValueError):
    pass


def _require(condition, message: str) -> None:
    if not condition:
        raise RegistryError(message)


def _unique_object(pairs):
    obj = {}
    for key, value in pairs:
        _require(key not in obj, f"duplicate JSON key: {key}")
        obj[key] = value
    return obj


def _reject_constant(value):
    raise RegistryError(f"nonfinite JSON constant: {value}")


def read_json(path: Path):
    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"),
                      object_pairs_hook=_unique_object, parse_constant=_reject_constant)


def literal_declarations(path: Path, names: set[str]) -> dict:
    """Read only module-level literals, without running even taxonomy imports."""
    found = {}
    for node in ast.parse(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), filename=str(path)).body:
        if isinstance(node, ast.Assign):
            targets = node.targets
        elif isinstance(node, ast.AnnAssign):
            targets = [node.target]
        else:
            continue
        for target in targets:
            if isinstance(target, ast.Name) and target.id in names:
                _require(target.id not in found, f"{path.name}: repeated {target.id}")
                try:
                    found[target.id] = ast.literal_eval(node.value)
                except (ValueError, TypeError) as exc:
                    raise RegistryError(f"{path.name}: {target.id} must be a literal") from exc
    return found


def published_declarations(root: Path = ROOT) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    """Conservative chooser coverage, including unprovisioned/refused datasets.

    `data.scaffolds.registry.published()` imports adapters and may omit an import failure.
    Reading KIND declarations covers every publishable source without loading a
    mixed task pool. A missing literal KIND retains the registry's fixture default.
    """
    return tuple(path.stem for path in sorted((root / "data" / "datasets").glob("*.py"))
                 if literal_declarations(path, {"KIND"}).get("KIND") == "dataset")


def _fields(obj, expected: set[str], label: str) -> None:
    _require(isinstance(obj, dict), f"{label}: expected object")
    _require(set(obj) == expected, f"{label}: fields must be {sorted(expected)}")


def _text(value, label: str) -> None:
    _require(isinstance(value, str) and bool(value.strip()), f"{label}: empty text")


def _unique_strings(values, label: str) -> None:
    _require(isinstance(values, list), f"{label}: expected list")
    for value in values:
        _text(value, label)
    _require(len(set(values)) == len(values), f"{label}: duplicates")


def _evidence(items, root: Path, label: str) -> None:
    _require(isinstance(items, list) and bool(items), f"{label}: evidence required")
    kinds = []
    for item in items:
        _fields(item, {"kind", "path", "locator"}, label)
        kind, path = item["kind"], item["path"]
        _text(path, label)
        _text(item["locator"], label)
        _require(kind in {"repository", "external-reference"}, f"{label}: evidence kind")
        kinds.append(kind)
        if kind == "external-reference":
            # This is a historical citation, not a file we have verified or may open.
            _require(Path(path).is_absolute(), f"{label}: external reference must be absolute")
            continue
        rel = Path(path)
        _require(not rel.is_absolute() and ".." not in rel.parts,
                 f"{label}: repository evidence must stay inside root")
        source = root / rel
        _require(source.resolve().is_relative_to(root.resolve()), f"{label}: evidence escapes root")
        _require(source.is_file(), f"{label}: missing evidence {path}")
        _require(item["locator"] in source.read_text(encoding="utf-8"),
                 f"{label}: stale evidence locator in {path}")
RETURN 0

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""`alf` -- the command line the agent uses to act in an ALFWorld TextWorld episode.

There is no chat and no interactive user. The agent is inside a text adventure, and the
only way to act is to run a command:

    python3 alf look            print the current observation and the admissible actions
    python3 alf do "<action>"   apply one action and print what happened
    python3 alf done            declare the episode over and stop acting
    python3 alf help            print this text

Why a CLI, and why a log
------------------------
The platform runs a task by starting a harness and then grading whatever it left
behind. An interactive benchmark has no single artifact to grade -- the *episode* is
the artifact -- so the task is turned into one: every action the agent takes goes
through this command, and every action is appended to `alf_actions.jsonl`. The check
then replays that log from the beginning in a fresh environment and reads the
environment's own `won` flag. Nothing this program prints is trusted as a verdict; the
log holds actions, and actions are re-executed rather than believed.

Why the log is the state
------------------------
TextWorld environments cannot be pickled, and a fresh `alf` process starts with a fresh
interpreter, so each invocation rebuilds the episode and replays the log up to the
current action. That is O(n^2) in the number of steps, and it is deliberate: it is the
only design in which the environment the agent sees and the environment the check
replays are built by *exactly the same code path*, which is what makes "the same
trajectory" a fact rather than a hope. With a 30-step budget the cost is a few tens of
seconds for a whole episode.

What the log looks like (one JSON object per line):
    {"kind": "action", "step": 0, "action": "go to desk 2"}
    {"kind": "done", "step": 4}

Where things live (override with the matching flag):
    <workdir>/game.tw-pddl        the game, put there by the platform's `SETUP`
    <workdir>/alf_actions.jsonl   this program's append-only output
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import os
import sys
from pathlib import Path

# `gym_env.py` is copied in beside this file by the dataset's `SETUP`. Import it from
# this file's own directory rather than the current directory, so `alf` works no matter
# where the harness invokes it from.
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import gym_env  # noqa: E402


def _fail(message: str, code: int = 2) -> "int":
    print(f"error: {message}", file=sys.stderr)
    return code


def _actions(records: list[dict]) -> list[str]:
    """The actions in a log, in order. Anything that is not an action is skipped."""
    return [str(r["action"]) for r in records
            if r.get("kind") == "action" and isinstance(r.get("action"), str)]


def _append(log: Path, record: dict) -> None:
    """Append one JSON record. A log that cannot be written must fail loudly.

    Unlike tau-bench's CLI -- where the log is evidence beside a database that is the
    real state -- here the log *is* the state. A silently dropped append would make the
    episode the agent saw and the episode the check replays diverge, so this raises
    rather than swallowing the error.
    """
    log.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with log.open("a") as handle:
        handle.write(json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")


def _print_state(state: dict, budget: int, steps: int, won: bool, done: bool) -> None:
    print(state["observation"].strip())
    print()
    print("Admissible actions:")
    for command in state["admissible"]:
        print(f"  {command}")
    print()
    status = "SOLVED" if won else ("episode over" if done else "in progress")
    print(f"[alf] step {steps}/{budget}; {status}")
    if done and not won:
        print("[alf] TextWorld ended the episode without the goal; the run continues to "
              "count only if no earlier step satisfied it.")
    _print_state(last, args.budget, len(actions) + 1, state["won"], last["done"])
    return 0


def cmd_done(args) -> int:
    """Mark the episode finished. Recorded; it is not an action and is not replayed."""
    records = gym_env.action_log_records(args.log)
    if not any(r.get("kind") == "done" for r in records):
        _append(args.log, {"kind": "done", "step": len(_actions(records))})
    print("Noted. The episode is marked done.")
    return 0


def _parse_global(argv: list) -> tuple[dict, list]:
    """Pull the path/budget flags out wherever they appear; leave the subcommand."""
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(add_help=False)
    parser.add_argument("--workdir", default=os.environ.get("ALFWORLD_WORKDIR", "."))
    parser.add_argument("--game", default=None)
    parser.add_argument("--log", default=None)
    parser.add_argument("--budget", type=int, default=gym_env.STEP_BUDGET)
    known, rest = parser.parse_known_args(argv)
    workdir = Path(known.workdir)
    known.game = Path(known.game) if known.game else workdir / gym_env.GAME_FILENAME
    known.log = Path(known.log) if known.log else workdir / gym_env.LOG_FILENAME
    return known, rest


USAGE = """alf -- act in an ALFWorld text episode

  python3 alf look            show the current observation and admissible actions
  python3 alf do "<action>"   apply one action; it costs one of the {budget} steps
  python3 alf done            declare the episode over

Options (anywhere on the line): --workdir DIR --game FILE --log FILE --budget N
The game is {game}; the action log is {log}.
""".format(budget=gym_env.STEP_BUDGET, game=gym_env.GAME_FILENAME,
           log=gym_env.LOG_FILENAME)


def main(argv: list) -> int:
    args, rest = _parse_global(list(argv))
    if not rest or rest[0] in ("-h", "--help", "help"):
        print(USAGE)
        return 0
    command, rest = rest[0], rest[1:]
    if command == "look":
        args.action = None
        return cmd_look(args)
    if command == "do":
        args.action = rest
        return cmd_do(args)
    if command == "done":
        return cmd_done(args)
    print(f"error: unknown command {command!r}", file=sys.stderr)

def cmd_look(args) -> int:
    """Print the current observation. Not an action: it does not consume the budget."""
    records = gym_env.action_log_records(args.log)
    actions = _actions(records)
    if not args.game.is_file():
        return _fail(f"no game file at {args.game}")
    state = gym_env.replay(str(args.game), actions, args.budget)
    if not state["records"] and actions:
        return _fail("the log names actions but none replayed")
    if state["records"]:
        last = state["records"][-1]
        current = {"observation": last["observation"],
                   "admissible": last["admissible"]}
        done = last["done"]
    else:
        current = {"observation": state["initial_observation"],
                   "admissible": state["initial_admissible"]}
        done = False
    _print_state(current, args.budget, len(actions), state["won"], done)
    return 0


def cmd_do(args) -> int:
    """Apply one action, append it to the log, and print the result."""
    if not args.action:
        return _fail('usage: alf do "<action>"')
    action = " ".join(args.action).strip()
    if not action:
        return _fail('usage: alf do "<action>"')
    if not args.game.is_file():
        return _fail(f"no game file at {args.game}")
    records = gym_env.action_log_records(args.log)
    actions = _actions(records)
    if len(actions) >= args.budget:
        print(f"error: the episode budget of {args.budget} actions is spent; the "
              f"episode is over. Use `alf look` to see the final state.", file=sys.stderr)
        return 2
    state = gym_env.replay(str(args.game), actions + [action], args.budget)
    if len(state["records"]) <= len(actions):
        return _fail(f"the environment did not accept a step for {action!r}")
    _append(args.log, {"kind": "action", "step": len(actions), "action": action})
    last = state["records"][-1]
    _print_state(last, args.budget, len(actions) + 1, state["won"], last["done"])
    return 0


def cmd_done(args) -> int:
    """Mark the episode finished. Recorded; it is not an action and is not replayed."""
                 f"{label}: stale evidence locator in {path}")
    _require("repository" in kinds, f"{label}: repository observation required")


def _blocker(blocker, root: Path, label: str) -> None:
    _fields(blocker, {"id", "family", "status", "observed", "evidence", "next_action"}, label)
    for field in ("id", "observed", "next_action"):
        _text(blocker[field], f"{label}.{field}")
    _require(blocker["family"] in FAMILIES, f"{label}: unknown blocker family")
    _require(blocker["status"] in STATUSES, f"{label}: unknown status")
    _evidence(blocker["evidence"], root, label)


def validate(document: dict, *, root: Path = ROOT) -> dict:
    _fields(document, {"schema_version", "review", "shared_blockers", "datasets"}, "registry")
    _require(type(document["schema_version"]) is int and document["schema_version"] == 1,
             "unsupported schema version")
    _fields(document["review"], {"date", "scope", "production_authorized", "notes"}, "review")
    _text(document["review"]["date"], "review.date")
    _require(document["review"]["scope"] == "repository-source-and-journal-only", "review scope")
    _require(document["review"]["production_authorized"] is False, "registry cannot authorize production")
    _unique_strings(document["review"]["notes"], "review.notes")

    taxonomy = literal_declarations(root / "tools/dataset_classes.py", {"CAPABILITY", "EXERCISES"})
    published = set(published_declarations(root))
    _require(set(taxonomy["CAPABILITY"]) == published, "taxonomy/published declarations differ")
RETURN 0
